Tableau Next
Tableau Next is agentic analytics that delivers personalized, contextual, and actionable insights
everywhere work happens.
As Salesforce’s end-to-end, agentic analytics experience, Tableau Next integrates the #1 analytics
platform with Agentforce’s context-aware AI and the Data 360 semantic layer that unifies business
knowledge across your organization. Designed to be composable and API-first, Tableau Next empowers
you to create, share, and reuse analytics assets seamlessly. Build and deliver personalized, contextual,
and actionable insights for every user, backed by Salesforce’s enterprise-grade performance, security, and
scalability.

About Tableau Next
Learn about Tableau Next editions, limits, guidelines, billable usage types, and terminology.
Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Tableau Next is the composable, AI-analytics platform that turns any type of data into actionable
insights. Tableau Next requires Data 360 and the Data 360 semantic layer.
Agentforce for Analytics
Learn about setting up and using Agentforce for Analytics. Prepare your data in Data Cloud, enable
Agentforce capabilities and create an agent with the Analytics and Visualization template, and turn
data into insights faster with Concierge, Data Pro, and Inspector.
Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
The Tableau Next analytics home page offers a quick way to build analytics assets, organize them
within workspaces and access the resources and assets available to you.
Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
You can access data for creating visualizations and dashboards by connecting to data directly (Data
360 DMOs and CIOs), by connecting to a semantic model that already has a data connection, or by
uploading your own data from CSV files. Use Tableau Semantics within Tableau Next to create a
semantic model and semantic calculated dimensions and measures.
Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
Explore data in a semantic model and surface insights that you can act on by building visualizations on
related data in your workspace to draw conclusions and make business decisions. Take data
visualization to the next level with dashboards, where you can add interactivity, enhance data
exploration, and provide a clear overview of the critical information that you need in one place.
Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Share your workspaces and assets with other users. Embed Tableau Next visualizations, metrics,
followed metrics, and dashboards into Lightning pages. Copy Tableau Next component code to
embed Tableau Next assets into third-party web apps. Transform your dashboards into standardized
templates to list on Marketplace, empowering collaboration, promoting standardization, and reducing
duplicate efforts across teams. Deploy your Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards
from your sandbox org into your production org using change sets and data kits.
Tableau Next Apps for Salesforce
Provide your teams with curated paths through your data, plus powerful tools for spontaneous, deep
explorations. With a Tableau Next App for Salesforce, you have a purpose-built set of analyses and
insights about a specific area of your business. Answer pressing questions, predict outcomes, and get
2

Tableau Next
recommendations on the next best actions with insights infused directly within the flow of work in
Salesforce.
Work with Tableau Next
Access Tableau Next metrics, visualizations, and dashboards for effective, data-based collaborations
with your team. Customize and personalize Tableau Next on your home page by filtering and following
metrics. Explore and refine metrics and other analytics assets to show you the insights most relevant
for your work.

Tableau Next Considerations, Guidelines, and Limits
Considerations are factors to understand before working with Tableau Next. Guidelines define best-
practice recommendations to improve your use of Tableau Next for optimal performance. Limits are
boundaries beyond which features are unavailable or usage billing charges are applied.
Billing Considerations for Tableau Next
Tableau Next is built on Data 360 and generative AI services. Usage of certain Tableau Next features
impacts credit consumption. Credit consumption can be either Data Services or Flex Credits. In
addition, many Tableau Next features are available on an unmetered basis. Unmetered usage does not
consume credits.
About Unmetered Tableau Next
With the Tableau Next unmetered permission, certain analytics features are available for unmetered
usage, including most queries and pre-configured AI agent usage. Unmetered usage doesn’t consume
credits.
AI and Data Usage in Tableau Next
As a Tableau Next customer, it’s important for you to know what data is being used in the features
where you’ve enabled AI.
Tableau Next Features on Companion Orgs
Use the Tableau Next app to access features in your Data Cloud One home org and companion orgs.
Tableau Next Glossary
Familiarize yourself with Tableau Next terminology.

Tableau Next Considerations, Guidelines, and Limits
Considerations are factors to understand before working with Tableau Next. Guidelines define best-
practice recommendations to improve your use of Tableau Next for optimal performance. Limits are
boundaries beyond which features are unavailable or usage billing charges are applied.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Localization Limitations
Tableau Next is localized with these exceptions.
•
Tableau Next offers limited language support. For a complete list of supported languages, see the
Analytics Cloud section of User Interface Language Support.
•
Included apps, metric insights, and Agentforce features aren’t multilingually supported.
Tableau Next provides internationalization support with these exceptions.
•
To see translated labels, set both the locale and language.
•
Data sources aren't modified for localization or internationalization.
•
Data sources can have a single locale specified in the metadata, including data models and process
definitions. Their locale isn’t overridden by individual user locale settings.

Billing Considerations for Tableau Next
Tableau Next is built on Data 360 and generative AI services. Usage of certain Tableau Next features
impacts credit consumption. Credit consumption can be either Data Services or Flex Credits. In addition,
many Tableau Next features are available on an unmetered basis. Unmetered usage does not consume
credits.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD  EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Unmetered Consumption Available
Data Queries, Data Transforms, and Analytics Actions of Agentforce initiated from Tableau Next are
unmetered when the interacting user has the Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and
Agentic Analytics workloads permission. For more information, see About Unmetered Tableau Next.
Consumption with the Data Services Digital Wallet Card
If your org has any active Data Services consumption cards, credits are consumed from the Data Services
card for Data 360 services. If you use up your Data Services credits but have Flex Credits, Data 360
services begin consuming Flex Credits.
Use Digital Wallet to check your current credit usage. The cost of each credit is determined by your
contract. To determine multipliers for each usage type, see the consumption card's rate card.

•

About Unmetered Tableau Next
With the Tableau Next unmetered permission, certain analytics features are available for unmetered
usage, including most queries and pre-configured AI agent usage. Unmetered usage doesn’t consume
credits.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Data Queries, Data Transforms, and Analytics Actions of Agentforce initiated from Tableau Next are
unmetered when the interacting user has the Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and
Agentic Analytics workloads permission.
To classify a Tableau Next interaction as unmetered usage, these conditions must be met.
22

Tableau Next About Tableau Next
•
The session must be tied to an identifiable user session or user action.
•
The user account must have the Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and Agentic Analytics
workloads permission.
•
For Data 360 queries and zero-copy services, users must have Data 360 Flex Credits. For more
information, see Billing Considerations for Tableau Next.
Unmetered Usage
These categories of services and features are available for unmetered usage.
Tableau Next Functional Area Available Unmetered Service or Feature
Intelligent Apps Installation Automated Salesforce setup and app installation for Tableau Next
Apps.
Batch Data Transforms
In the home org only: Transform operations on Data 360 data used by
Tableau Next, initiated from Data 360 or Tableau Next. Within their
contexts, users can connect and preview data tables.
Note: Batch Data Transforms are unmetered for all users in an
unmetered org.
Tableau Next Authoring
Semantic queries: This covers semantic queries generated through
pre-built apps or cloud-built Lightning Web Components, for example,
Marketing Cloud via Connect API.
Semantic layer authoring: Analysts can run queries as part of
authoring semantic models within Data 360 or Tableau Next.
Developer experiences: Developers can use the Tableau Next
Embedding SDK and semantic queries via the Tableau Next REST
Connect API.
Data Lens: Users can open a Data Lens within Tableau Next and
explore it with queries run within their contexts.
Query services: Direct access query services within Home, Data Cloud
One, or Personal Org. Users can run dashboards, metrics, insights, and
data lens queries on objects in a home or production org.
Note: Uploading CSV or Excel files to a Tableau Next workspace isn’t
unmetered.

visualizations, metrics, and insights for analytics embedded in

Salesforce Clouds.
Custom Tableau Next Assets: Analysts can create and edit
dashboards, visualizations, and metrics in Tableau Next. Assets
created in Tableau Next can be viewed and consumed anywhere in
Salesforce or externally.
Business User Home: Explore metrics, insights, and Insight Summary
in the Tableau Next business user Home. Metrics and Insight service
generate queries in user context, both within Tableau Next or
anywhere in Salesforce via Tab.
In-the-Flow of Work: Explore dashboards, visualizations, metrics,

Agentforce for Analytics
Tableau Next users can work with the Analytics and Visualization agent
with these pre-configured topics:
Concierge: Converse with the agent about your data.
Inspector: Set proactive alerts to get notified when thresholds are
met.
Data Pro: Get assistance with Tableau Semantics, including semantic
model optimization, semantic descriptions, related fields, calculated
fields, etc.
Actionability Take action from Tableau Next assets with Lightning Flows via use of
semantic queries in Lightning Flow Builder.

Tableau Next Glossary
Familiarize yourself with Tableau Next terminology.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Asset
Content or workspace inside Tableau Next. Assets include data objects, semantic models,
metrics, visualizations, dashboards, workspaces.
Calculated Dimension
A custom dimension created in Tableau Semantics to apply specific logic to existing data. It helps
classify, group, and refine dimension values without altering the raw data. Calculated during
queries, it can be used in reports, pivot tables, or dashboards. For example, Full Name =
[First Name] + " " + [Last Name].
Calculated Insight
A tool in Data 360 that queries, transforms, and creates complex calculations based on stored
data. It helps identify and highlight significant patterns and insights.
Calculated Measure
A numerical custom calculation in Tableau Semantics that generates values based on existing
data. It's computed at run time and can be a simple expression or complex code. It includes
metadata like default aggregation, data type, and aggregation type, and can be used in
dashboards, reports, or visualizations. For example Price =
[ItemCost]*[Quantity]*(1-[Discount]).
Calculated Insight Object (CIO)
A data model object created after a calculated insight is processed. You can connect to an
existing CIO in Data 360 and add it as a data asset in your workspace.
Comma-Separated Values (CSV)
A text file that stores data in tabular format. CSV files are commonly used to move data between
28

Tableau Next About Tableau Next
applications and programs. Similar terms include text file or flat file.
Dashboard
A curated set of metrics, visualizations, and filters intended to analyze related information. Often
interactive and designed to answer a specific data analysis question.
Data
A collection of values or information, or a piece of information. Data fields can be dimensions or
measures and can take many forms such as text or numbers.
Data 360
A product used to organize and unify data across Salesforce and other external data sources.
After data has been ingested into Data 360, it can be manipulated and filtered into audience
segments that can be used in activation targets, like Marketing Cloud Engagement.
Data Lake Object (DLO)
A storage container within the data lake for the data ingested into all data streams within Data
360. It serves as a repository for the transformed data stored in the lake.
Data Model Object (DMO)
A grouping of data stored in Data 360. A DMO is made up of attributes created from data
streams, insights, and other sources. This can be raw data or transformed data, depending on
how the data was initially added and processed.
Data Stream
A data source brought into Data 360. See also: DMO.
Data Source
Any system you can obtain data from, such as a database, API, flat file, or streaming data
platform.
Dimension
Qualitative data field that usually contains categorical data, such as Product Category, Lead
Status, and Case Subject. Dimensions are handy for grouping and filtering your data. Unlike
measures, you can’t perform math on dimensions. See also: Measure.
Logical View
A data object that combines multiple tables using special joins. It allows you to query the
enriched dataset as a single object, with its own fields, and can be used in relationships,
calculated fields, metrics, and other semantic definitions.
Insight
A driver, trend, contributor, or outlier that Tableau Next automatically detects for metrics.
29

Tableau Next About Tableau Next
Insights highlight and describe hidden changes or anomalies in metrics.
Measure
Quantitative data field that contains numerical data like revenue and exchange rate. You can do
math on measures, such as calculating the total revenue and the minimum exchange rate. See
also: Dimension.
Metric
Business key performance indicators (KPIs) used to track, compare, and assess performance or
business processes over time. A metric is derived from one or more measures and a date field.
Metrics are built from Semantic Models.
Personal Space
An isolated environment for analysts to work safely in a self-service manner without disturbing
other managed environments. Analysts can bring their own data, reuse assets from other places,
and perform their work independently.
Semantic Definition
The building blocks of Semantic Models, providing context and meaning to the data. They
include field metadata, Calculated Measures, Groups, Sets, and more.
Semantic Model
A collection of metadata that describes the data in a data object. It describes the grouping of
semantic definitions, and can also include metrics and calculated insights. Semantic models that
consist of multiple data objects also define the relationships between data objects.
Sub-Metrics
Detailed performance indicators that provide additional granularity to a main metric, such as
Total Revenue YTD or Average Monthly Close Rate.
Tableau Next
The composable, AI-analytics platform for any data, enabled by Data 360 and the Salesforce
Platform.
Visualization
A visual representation of your data as a chart or graph. Visualizations are constructed from a
DMO or semantic model to explore or analyze data. You can combine visualizations from the
same workspace on a dashboard.
Viz
Short for a visualization.
30

Tableau Next About Tableau Next
Workspace
A collaborative environment in Tableau Next for analysts to create and organize analytic assets to
answer questions about their data. Assets must be created in a workspace and can be reused
across multiple workspaces.

Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Tableau Next is the composable, AI-analytics platform that turns any type of data into actionable
insights. Tableau Next requires Data 360 and the Data 360 semantic layer.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Note As of October 14, 2025, Data Cloud has been rebranded to Data 360. During this transition,
you may see references to Data Cloud in our application and documentation. While the name is
new, the functionality and content remains unchanged.
View supported editions.
Required tasks
To enable Tableau Next
Set Up Data 360 for Tableau Next
Create Semantic Layer
Create the Tableau Next Admin
Enable Tableau Next
To provision users for Tableau Next
Assign a User a Tableau Next Permission Set
Optional Tasks
To enable GenAI and Agent features
Enable Einstein Generative AI for Tableau Next
Enable Semantics AI in Tableau Next (Beta)
Set Up Agentforce for Analytics
To enable Tableau Next integration
Enable Slack for Tableau Next
32

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Optional Tasks
Enable Metric Insight Summaries in Tableau Next
Set Up Data 360 for Tableau Next
Set up Data 360 for Tableau Next to enable real-time data access and insights in your visualizations.
Tableau Next requires Data 360 for data ingestion and data queries. Before setting up Tableau Next,
configure Data 360 and create a Data Model Object for your org.
Data Governance in Tableau Next
Tableau Next follows the data governance permissions and policies defined in Data 360. Access to
data in Data 360 is controlled first by data spaces, then by policies. Permission sets grant access to
data spaces. Policies allow or deny access to data at an object, field, or record level.
Enable Einstein Generative AI for Tableau Next
Einstein Generative AI provides summaries and automated visuals in Tableau Next. Tableau requires
Einstein generative AI and the Einstein Trust Layer to access AI features. You also need to turn on
Einstein generative AI data collection to save feedback and auditing data.
Set Up Agentforce for Analytics
Configure users, turn on organization features, and create and activate an agent for analytics and
visualizations in Tableau Next.
Set Up Agent Scoping for Concierge
Agent Scoping defines the list of semantic models that an analytics agent can search when a user asks
a question in Concierge: Analytics Q&A with no clear context. For example, Concierge may lack context
when it's used in Slack or when it's embedded in a page without Tableau Next assets such as a metric
or dashboard.
Create the Tableau Next Admin
To access the Tableau Next Administration page, and to enable Tableau Next in your organization,
users require the Tableau Unmetered Admin or Tableau Next Admin permission set.
Enable Tableau Next
When you enable Tableau Next for your org, you can also enable other integrated features.
Assign a User a Tableau Next Permission Set
Assign permissions to users based on their level of interaction with Tableau Next capabilities. Users
must already be created in Salesforce. You can’t add users in the Tableau Next app. Each Tableau Next
permission set that you assign to a user consumes a license. You can view your license status as you
assign permission sets.
Tableau Next Personal Org (Beta)
Tableau Next includes the Personal Org, an isolated environment designed to provide analysts with
self-service capabilities.
Enable Slack for Tableau Next
When enabled, this feature allows users to share Tableau Next links in Slack with a preview of the
metric. After an admin Connects Salesforce and Slack, previews sent in Slack are displayed according
to the senders’ permission access that is governed by Tableau Next. And this seamless integration
between Slack and Tableau Next requires no additional apps. From Slack or Tableau Next, users can
33

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
view related conversations that they have access to about Tableau Next analytics that have been
shared in Slack.
Enable Agentforce for Analytics in Slack
When enabled, your users can open Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Slack.
They can then ask questions in context about metrics and dashboards that have been shared to Slack
from Tableau Next. The analytics agent runs a query allowed based on the user's permissions, and
analyzes questions based on the data for the metric and the semantic model enabled for the agent in
Tableau Next.
Enable Metric Insight Summaries in Tableau Next
Tableau Metric Insight Summaries give users a personalized overview of the most important insights
from the metrics that they care about. The Metric Insight Summaries feature is built natively into
Tableau Next and is independent of the Agentforce platform. The summary is generated based on the
metrics that a user follows and is displayed on the Following page in Tableau Next. Only users with
permission can access the metric summaries.
Enable Semantics AI in Tableau Next (Beta)
Use Semantics AI to generate calculated fields and to suggest relationships.
Enable Q&A Calibration for Concierge (Beta)
When Q&A Calibration is enabled in Tableau Next, analysts can test, calibrate, and improve analytics
agent responses to questions in Concierge.
Enable Template Builder in Tableau Next (Beta)
Template builder lets users transform dashboards into standardized templates that can be listed in the
Marketplace, empowering collaboration, promoting standardization, and reducing duplicate efforts
across teams.
Enable Metrics Templates in Tableau Next (Beta)
Metrics templates provide users with pre-defined metrics in the Tableau Next Marketplace, allowing
them to deploy industry-standard and best-practice metrics rapidly without the extensive
development and configuration overhead associated with custom creation.
Enable Beta Connectors in Tableau Next

Data Governance in Tableau Next
Tableau Next follows the data governance permissions and policies defined in Data 360. Access to data
in Data 360 is controlled first by data spaces, then by policies. Permission sets grant access to data
spaces. Policies allow or deny access to data at an object, field, or record level.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Note As of October 14, 2025, Data Cloud has been rebranded to Data 360. During this transition,
you may see references to Data Cloud in our application and documentation. While the name is
new, the functionality and content remains unchanged.
View supported editions.
Learn About Data Governance
Review these concepts before implementing data governance.
Know Your Audience
When planning your implementation, determine what data your Tableau Next users will need to access.
Work with your Salesforce Administrator and your Data Cloud Architect to create and assign the custom
permissions and permission sets and implement the data tags and policies you'll need.
Governance Basics
Data access is built in layers.
35

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Permissions
Permissions grant access to data spaces. This foundational layer ensures that only users with
assigned permissions can access a data space and its contents.
Policies
Policies allow and deny access to objects, fields, and records within data spaces. Policies are
created by choosing whether the policy allows or denies access, selecting the target data
(objects, fields, or records), and identifying a group of users by defining criteria based on custom
permissions or user attributes. A deny policy will override an allow policy.
For example, a user who is allowed to see the Payment object can see all the fields on Payment.
If a deny policy is applied to the Credit Card field on the Payment object, that user won't be able
to see the Credit Card field or its contents.
Sharing
Sharing an asset within Tableau Next allows another user to view or edit it but doesn't override
the permissions and policies that apply to that user.
Governance Concepts
Familiarize yourself with data governance terminology.
Attribute-Based Access Control (ABAC)
Allows or denies access to data based on attributes of the user, the data, or the environment.
Allow All Policy
On by default in Data 360, this policy allows access to all objects in a data space for all users. An
admin needs to disable this policy before creating granular governance controls.
Data Tagging
Labels attached to data assets (objects, fields, and records) that can be used in policies to
control access to the tagged asset.
Field-Level Security (FLS)
Controls access to specific fields on objects within data spaces.
Object-Level Security (OLS)
Controls access to objects within data spaces.
Record-Level Security (RLS)
Controls access to individual records in an object. RLS can be expanded with joins to other data
sources, such as Manager, Role, and Territory hierarchies, to manage access based on other
criteria, like vertical reporting chains, horizontal roles, or regional associations.
36

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)
Defined as part of a permission set and controls which data spaces and which objects and fields
within those data spaces users can access.
Users with View All Records or Modify All Records and Deny Policies
A user with one of these powerful permissions can see object and field metadata, even if a deny
policy restricts their access to the data in that object or field. This allows them to interact with
the objects and fields in semantic models, visualizations, metrics, and other places without
seeing the contents.
User Experience
A Tableau Next user will see only data assets they have been granted access to through permissions and
policies in Data 360 and have sharing access to in Tableau Next. If a user doesn’t have access to a data
asset it won’t appear in semantic models, visualizations, metrics, or other areas of the Tableau Next
interface.
Using Data Governance
Different approaches to data visibility can be built and layered with Data Governance policies.
See Also
Salesforce Help: Data Governance in Data 360
Salesforce Help: Object, Field, and Record Level Security

Apply Data 360 Object-Level Security with Role-based Access Control
Grant access to Data 360 objects, such as data lake objects (DLOs) and data model objects (DMOs), by
creating a custom permission, adding it to a custom permission set, and assigning that permission set to
users.
See Also
Salesforce Help: Associate a Permission Set with a Data Space
Salesforce Help: Create a Role-Based Access Control Policy in Data 360
Apply Record-Level Security with User Attribute
To allow access based on the current user, create an Allow policy matching a field from the object to a
field from the user's record.
Example Allow access to record owner.Define a policy that allows access if the object's Owner
matches the user's ID. Apply the policy to all users.Users will be able to see records that they own.
See Also
Salesforce Help: Create a Record-Level Policy in Data 360
Apply Record-Level Security with an Entitlement Table
Some organizations use an entitlement table to limit access to data associated with a particular record to
a specific group of users. Entitlement tables are useful for many-to-many relationships that are not
present in built-in hierarchies (Role, Manager, Territory) or that are easily described in user attributes.
EEnnttiittlleemmeenntt ttaabbllee ssttrruuccttuurree
An entitlement table in Data Governance needs one row for each record-user association, using the
15-character record and user IDs. For example

001aa000000CCC3 005aa000000Usr2

•

44.. Set the condition to match the User ID in the entitlement table to the logged-in user's ID.
This restricts the list of object IDs to those associated with the logged-in user's ID, then allows
visibility on those object IDs and related data.

Semantic Built-In AI This accelerates and streamlines semantic model
creation and maintenance.
Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta) This enables automated checks on semantic data
models to detect optimization opportunities.
This tool surfaces issues with guided resolutions
to improve model quality and reliability for AI-
powered agents and analytics.

entities with AI. Accurate descriptions boost
agent response accuracy. Be sure to review and
edit suggestions before saving.

menu to test questions in Concierge, and then
verify and calibrate results.

Set Up Agent Scoping for Concierge
Agent Scoping defines the list of semantic models that an analytics agent can search when a user asks a
question in Concierge: Analytics Q&A with no clear context. For example, Concierge may lack context
when it's used in Slack or when it's embedded in a page without Tableau Next assets such as a metric or
dashboard.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Admins can use Agent Scoping to define a secure, approved list of semantic models for each analytics
agent that uses the pre-built Data Analysis subagent with the Analyze Data action. The list of approved
semantic models defined for an agent is called a fallback list.
The agent can be based on the Analytics and Visualization template or a custom agent based on the
Agentforce Employee Agent template.
Analysts can view the fallback list but can't edit it.
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To create and edit the fallback list: Tableau Unmetered Admin or Tableau Next Admin
permission set
To view the fallback list: Tableau Unmetered Platform or Self-Service
Analyst or Tableau Next Platform or Self-Service
Analyst permission set
11.. In Tableau Next home, click Agent Scoping.
22.. Select the agent you want to configure. Only active agents in the org that use the Data Analysis topic
are available.
Agents can be based on the Analytics and Visualization agent template or a custom agent based on
the Agentforce Employee Agent template.
33.. Click Select Models.
44.. In the Select Semantic Models dialog box, select each semantic model (up to 10 models allowed),
and then click Select. Use the Data Space search to filter the view to semantic models in that data
space.
55.. Confirm that the models you selected are in the list of semantic models when you select that agent in
the Agent Scoping page.
To view details about a model, select the model name in the list, click the arrow, and then click
Details. You might need to scroll to the right to see the arrow. To remove a model from the list, select
the model name in the list, click the arrow, and thenTo remove a model from the list, select the model
43

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
name in the list, click the arrow, and then click Remove.
Requirements
•
Fallback lists of semantic models can only be created for active agents that use the Data Analysis
subagent with the Analyze Data action. The active agents can be based on the Analytics and
Visualization agent template or a custom agent based on the Agentforce Employee Agent template.
•
The agent must be added to the Agent Access permission set to be available. For more information,
see Manage Employee Agent Access.
•
Semantic models added to the fallback list must belong to the same org as the agent being
configured. At least one active analytics agent must be available in the same org to configure a fallback
list.
Limitations and Considerations
•
You can add any number of semantic models to the fallback list, however selecting 10 or fewer
models improves agent accuracy.
•
Each fallback list is associated with a specific agent. Adding a new analytics agent to the org doesn't
inherit another agent fallback list.
•
User permissions are always respected at runtime. If a user doesn't have access to a semantic model, it
won't be used for their queries even if it's on the fallback list.
•
When a user asks questions in Concierge with available context in a dashboard or metric, the agent
uses the semantic models associated with the dashboard or metric instead of the fallback list of
approved models.
•
For Home and Governed orgs, one fallback list can be configured per agent. For Personal orgs, the
fallback list can be configured per agent for semantic models available in the Personal org only.

app. Each Tableau Next permission set that you assign to a user consumes a license. You can view your

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
1. For users that have an old Tableau Next permission set, go to Setup > Users > Permission Set
Assignments. Click Edit Assignments, select the old permission set, click Remove, and click Save.
2. From the App Launcher, find and select Tableau Next.
3. In the Tableau Next app, click Administration.
4. Under User Management, click Users.
5. In the Users pane, click Add. You can view the permissions included in each permission set on the
User Management page of Tableau Next Administration.
6. On the Add Users page, click Provision Existing Users. The users who aren’t assigned Tableau Next
permission sets are listed.
7. Select the users that you want to assign Tableau Next licenses to, and click Next.
8. Select the user that you want to assign permission sets to. You can select multiple users if you want to
bulk assign the same permission set to a set of users. Click Assign Permission Set to specify a Tableau
Next permission set to the user(s) you selected. You can view your license status as you assign
permission sets.
9. Review the assignments, and click Next.
10. Review your changes, and then click Add Users.
(Optional) Create and assign a custom permission set
If you want to provide users with unmetered access via a custom permission set, create your custom
permission set with the Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and Agentic Analytics
workloads permission, and then assign the permission set to the users.
Create a custom permission set:
1. From Setup, in the Quick Find box, enter permission sets, and then select Permission Sets.
2. Click New.
3. Enter a name for the label and click Save.
4. From the new permission set you created, on the System tab, click System Permissions.
5. Click Edit to edit the System Permissions. Select Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and
Agentic Analytics workloads from the list and save.
Add a custom permission set to a user:
1. From Setup, in the Quick Find box, enter users, and then select Users.

and click Save. Then go back to the Permission Set Assignments section, and click Edit Assignments
again.
5. Select your custom permission set that has the Access includes unmetered Analytics Insights and
Agentic Analytics workloads permission, and click Add.
6. Click Save.

These licenses contain everything in Tableau Next, including assigning permission sets to users, tracking

assets created from templates.

Tableau Next Personal Org (Beta)
Tableau Next includes the Personal Org, an isolated environment designed to provide analysts with self-
service capabilities.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
The Personal Org gives you a dedicated space for self-service analysis, allowing you to combine corporate
data with your own datasets and discover unique insights. Within the Personal Org, analysts can
incorporate their own data, utilize assets from other areas, and conduct analysis autonomously.
View supported editions.
Note Personal Org contains pilot or beta services that are subject to the Beta Services Terms at
Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer, and
applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of these pilot or beta services is at the
Customer's sole discretion.
A Personal Org is distinct from the Tableau Next production environment (also referred to as the
production org), which is a managed and governed environment. The Tableau Next production
environment is the space where finalized and approved content is deployed. The production
environment is controlled by administrators and is designed to maintain administrative control and
governance over the data and analysis. It serves as the source of trusted data for various operations
across Tableau Next and trusted spaces within your Salesforce org.
Considerations and Limitations
For a general list of considerations and limitations of Personal Org in the Tableau Next platform, see
Tableau Next Considerations, Guidelines, and Limits. The list here describes considerations and
limitations that are specific to this Beta version of Personal Org:
•
Access to assets in a Personal Org is controlled by Sharing and Attribute-Based Access Control (ABAC)
that is set in the production environment. See Data Governance in Tableau Next.
55

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
•
You can give access to Personal Orgs to users with the permission sets Tableau Next Platform Analyst,
or Tableau Next Self-Service Analyst . However, users with Tableau Next Consumer permission set
cannot access or own Personal Orgs.
•
Personal Orgs that are created in this Beta release are temporary. You will not be able to migrate or
export Beta Personal Orgs to future releases.
•
We support a maximum of one Personal Org per user with a Creator license. The total number of
Personal Orgs allowed in an organization is limited to the lower of: 100 or the total number of Creator
licenses in the organization.
•
Only users who possess a Creator license can be provisioned a Personal Org and have access to it.
•
Personal Orgs are configured to only support the reuse of assets from the default data space. Similarly,
any new assets you create within your Personal Org can only be stored in the default data space.
•
There is currently no mechanism to move, export, or otherwise transfer content out of a Personal Org
and into a production environment.
Personal Org Capabilities in Shared Workspaces (Beta)
In Tableau Next, all of an analyst's data and resources reside within their Personal Org. This topic
details the feature and function capabilities related to Personal Orgs when a workspace is shared with
other analysts.
Enable Tableau Next Personal Orgs (Beta)
Tableau Next Personal Orgs allow your analysts to experiment with data and create content in a
dedicated environment. As an admin, use this process to activate the Personal Org feature for your
organization.
Create a Tableau Next Personal Org for an Analyst (Beta)
A Personal Org is an isolated environment designed to provide analysts with self-service capabilities,
reuse assets from other places, perform their work independently, and not affect other environments.
Delete Tableau Next Personal Org (Beta)
To remove a Personal Org when a user is offboarded or no longer requires access to Tableau Next,
follow these steps. Deleting a Personal Org is permanent: it will delete all associated content and
cannot be restored after the action is confirmed.

the feature and function capabilities related to Personal Orgs when a workspace is shared with other
analysts.

Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer, and
applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of these pilot or beta services is at the
Customer's sole discretion.

11.. From the App Launcher, find and select Tableau Next.

to access a Personal Org.

in to Tableau Next, they will see a Personal Org in the bottom left of their workspace.

Enable Slack for Tableau Next
When enabled, this feature allows users to share Tableau Next links in Slack with a preview of the metric.
After an admin Connects Salesforce and Slack, previews sent in Slack are displayed according to the
senders’ permission access that is governed by Tableau Next. And this seamless integration between
Slack and Tableau Next requires no additional apps. From Slack or Tableau Next, users can view related
conversations that they have access to about Tableau Next analytics that have been shared in Slack.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To enable Tableau Next: Tableau Unmetered Admin or Tableau Next Admin
permission set
For a streamlined setup, use the Salesforce Go feature for Tableau Next with Slack. Or follow these steps.
11.. Connect Slack to Salesforce.
22.. In Tableau Next, click Administration, and then click Settings.
33.. On the General page, enable Collaborate with Slack and Tableau Next.
Note Session Settings that restrict IPs impact the integration between Slack and Salesforce. To
resolve this, open Session Settings and confirm that Lock sessions to the IP address from which
they originated and Enforce login IP ranges on every request are not enabled.
See Also
Salesforce Help: Share Tableau Next Metrics in Slack
Salesforce Help: Enable Agentforce for Analytics in Slack
Enable Agentforce for Analytics in Slack
When enabled, your users can open Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Slack.
They can then ask questions in context about metrics and dashboards that have been shared to Slack
from Tableau Next. The analytics agent runs a query allowed based on the user's permissions, and
analyzes questions based on the data for the metric and the semantic model enabled for the agent in
Tableau Next.
60

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Available in: All Slack plans with a Salesforce Agentforce license
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To update Tableau Next settings and assign Tableau Next Admin permission set
permission sets:
To update Salesforce org settings: Salesforce Admin permission set
To update Slack settings: Slack Admin and Slack Organization Admin
permission set
Agentforce for Analytics in Slack requires a configured Tableau Next environment. For more information,
see Admin: Set Up Tableau Next.
Set Up Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Slack
To enable Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Slack, you'll need to perform setup
tasks or confirm settings in both Tableau Next and Slack. Before you begin, it's helpful to review the
general requirements and steps for enabling this feature.
11.. Salesforce and Slack must be connected, and then Slack must be enabled for Tableau Next. For more
information, see Connect Salesforce and Slack and Enable Slack for Tableau Next.
22.. Agentforce for Analytics and Concierge: Analytics Q&A must be enabled in Tableau Next Features
(Setup). For more information, see "Activate Agentforce for Analytics" in Set Up Agentforce for
Analytics.
To get started with enabling Agentforce for Analytics and the Analytics and Visualization template in
Tableau Next, contact your Salesforce representative.
33.. An agent must be built from the Analytics and Visualization template in Agent Builder, and the agent
must use the Data Analysis topic. For more information, see Enable the Data Analysis topic for
Analytics and Visualization in Tableau Next.
44.. Agentforce must be connected and enabled in Slack (covered in this topic).
55.. The agent created from the Analytics and Visualization template must be installed and enabled in
Slack (covered in this topic).
66.. A Tableau Next metric or dashboard must be shared in Slack. For more information, see Share Tableau
Next Metrics in Slack.
After Agentforce for Analytics is enabled in Slack, users can interact with it when they open a metric or
dashboard shared from Tableau Next in Slack.
When a user views a shared metric or dashboard preview in Slack, Slack searches for connected agents
that specifically meet the following criteria (listed below). The Ask Agentforce for Analytics button is then
displayed in the preview for agents that meet the criteria. When a use clicks Ask Agentforce for Analytics,
61

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
the relevant agent opens in Slack.
•
The Analytics and Visualization agent is installed in Slack and enabled in the workspace.
•
That agent uses the Data Analysis topic.
•
That agent is from the same Salesforce org that the metric or dashboard is shared from.
•
The Slack user has user permission to access the agent (defined by the Salesforce user agent
permission).
•
If multiple agents meet the previous criteria (multiple agents in Slack are qualified to answer questions
about the metric or dashboard), the first agent installed and activated in Slack opens for the user.
Configure Slack and Tableau Next
Tableau Next must be connected to Slack and enabled for collaboration.
Note Session Settings that restrict IPs impact the integration between Slack and Salesforce. To
resolve this, open Session Settings. Confirm that Lock sessions to the IP address from which they
originated and Enforce login IP ranges on every request aren't enabled.
A Slack Admin and Salesforce Admin need to perform these steps.
11.. In Slack, select the workspace space name, select Tools & settings, and then select Manage
Salesforce Organizations.
22.. On the Salesforce organizations page, select Connect Salesforce Org.
33.. Copy the org prefix from the URL for your Salesforce org, prepend it to my.salesforce.com, and
paste that URL in Salesforce org to connect to the Salesforce organizations page. For example,
uniqueOrgNamePrefix.my.salesforce.com.
44.. From the Choose a Slack user account field, select Email. This sets the method used for
authentication.
55.. Select Request Connection. This triggers a request for the Salesforce Admin to approve.
66.. In your Salesforce org, select Setup.
77.. In the Quick Find box, enter Slack Connection, and then select Manage Slack Connection. The
pending request from your Slack workspace will be displayed.
88.. From User Configuration, for Salesforce Mapping Field, select Email. Agree to all terms and then click
Approve.
99.. In Slack, refresh the Salesforce organizations page to see the approval for the Salesforce org
connection. Click your Salesforce org name in the list, and then click Activate.
1100.. In the Activate this connection window, confirm the activation method is correct, and then click
Activate again.
1111.. In the Salesforce Setup, in the Quick Find box, enter Tableau Next, then select Tableau Next
Features. Confirm that Collaborate with Slack and Tableau Next is enabled.
62

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Enable Agentforce for Analytics and Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau
Next
After connecting Slack and your Salesforce org and enabling collaboration with Slack and Tableau Next,
you’re ready to set up the agent.
•
Agentforce for Analytics and Concierge: Analytics Q&A must be enabled in Tableau Next Features
(Setup) to make the Analytics and Visualization template available as an Agentforce agent. To get
started with enabling Agentforce for Analytics and the Analytics and Visualization template in Tableau
Next, contact your Salesforce representative.
•
An agent must be built from the Analytics and Visualization template in Agent Builder, with the Data
Analysis topic added. For more information, see "Create an Agent with the Analytics and Visualization
Template" in Set Up Agentforce for Analytics and Enable the Data Analysis topic for Analytics and
Visualization in Tableau Next.
Enable Agentforce and Concierge: Analytics Q&A for Use in Slack
This step requires that your Salesforce org is already connected to Slack, and an Agentforce agent has
already been created in Salesforce from the Analytics and Visualization template, with the Data Analysis
topic added.
Select a pending connection following these steps.
11.. From the Setup page, enter Agents in the Quick Find box, then select Agentforce Agents.
22.. Click the name for the agent you created from the Analytics and Visualization template.
33.. In the Agent Details page, select Open in Builder.
44.. In the Agentforce Builder page, select Connections, and then select Add.
55.. In the Add external app window, for Connection Type, select API. For Integration Name, enter Slack.
For Connected App, enter Slack to search for it. Select Slack, and then select Save.
66.. If Slack is already set up as the connected app, use the existing connection. If a Slack connection
hasn't been set up, select Setup, search for oauth in Quick Find, and then select Connected Apps
Oauth Usage. Slack should be a suggested app. For Slack, click Install. In the Connected Apps Oauth
Usage page, verify that Slack is now installed.
77.. In the Connections page in Agentforce Builder, confirm that Slack is listed as a connection in the
Salesforce org.
Next, the Slack Organization Admin needs to approve the connection for the workspace in Slack.
88.. Select the workspace space name, select Tools & settings, and then select Manage Agentforce.
99.. In the Agentforce settings page, confirm the Analytics and Visualization agent is listed as Ready to
install. Select Review for that agent, and then click Allow.
1100.. In the Add Analytics and Visualization to a workspace window, select the workspaces that you want
to add the agent to, and then select Add to Workspaces. In the Agentforce page, click the Active
Agents tab to confirm the agent has been activated.
1111.. In the connected Slack workspace, the Ask Agentforce for Analytics button should now be available on
the metrics card menu in Slack for users who have permission to use this agent in the connected
Salesforce org.
63

Tableau Next Admin: Set Up Tableau Next
Test the Agent in Slack
To confirm the agent is available in Slack, you can open a shared metric or dashboard and ask questions
using Agentforce.
11.. Share a metric or dashboard in Slack.
22.. View a shared metric or dashboard in Slack.
33.. Select Ask Agentforce for Analytics in the metric or dashboard card menu. Then, use natural
language to ask Agentforce to analyze your data. For example, "Break down total sales this quarter by
product family."
44.. Or, in a thread that contains the shared metric or dashboard, mention the name of the agent followed
by a question, For example, "What product family is leading in total sales this year?"
For more examples of the types of questions you can ask the analytics agent, see About Concierge:
Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next.
Enable Metric Insight Summaries in Tableau Next
Tableau Metric Insight Summaries give users a personalized overview of the most important insights
from the metrics that they care about. The Metric Insight Summaries feature is built natively into Tableau
Next and is independent of the Agentforce platform. The summary is generated based on the metrics
that a user follows and is displayed on the Following page in Tableau Next. Only users with permission
can access the metric summaries.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To enable Metric Insight Summaries: Tableau Unmetered Admin or Tableau Next Admin
permission set

For more information, see About Metric Insight Summaries in Tableau Next.
Enable Metric Insight Summaries
11.. In Tableau Next, click Administration, and then click Settings.
22.. On the General page, enable Metric Insight Summaries.
When this setting is turned off, users still see descriptive and predictive insights associated with
individual metrics. Insights that don't require generative AI or LLM are enabled by default and aren't
affected by this setting.

55.. Click Save.

Agentforce for Analytics
Learn about setting up and using Agentforce for Analytics. Prepare your data in Data Cloud, enable
Agentforce capabilities and create an agent with the Analytics and Visualization template, and turn data
into insights faster with Concierge, Data Pro, and Inspector.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Agentforce for Analytics is a collection of pre-built agentic capabilities, Concierge, Data Pro, and
Inspector, that streamline data model creation and management, deliver personalized, contextual
insights, and proactively alert users about data conditions they care about.
Concierge: Analytics Q&A
Business users can ask freeform questions about analytics assets (such as metrics and
dashboards) based on a semantic model to get trusted answers from their data in rich text and
visualizations.
Data Pro: Preparation and Modeling
Analytics and data stewards can conversationally curate and create semantic objects and analytic
assets when authoring a semantic model.
Inspector: Proactive Alerts
Business users can conversationally set up alerts to trigger on the conditions they set on the
metrics they care about, and receive proactive and intelligent notifications on them.
How Concierge, Data Pro, and Inspector work together
Concierge provides users insights into your data conversationally. It needs well-structured and described
data to provide accurate and relevant insights. Data Pro helps analysts create well-defined semantic
models so your analytics agent can understand the data and answer questions with confidence.
Inspector uses Concierge’s data analysis to help users effortlessly set up contextual data alerts.
Concierge, Data Pro, and Inspector topics and actions are available through the Analytics and
Visualization template in Agentforce Studio. This Agentforce Employee Agent (AEA) template is a default,
pre-built framework provided by the Agentforce platform to help organizations quickly deploy agents for
their internal employees.
Enable Agentforce for Analytics, Concierge, Data Pro, and Inspector in Tableau Next to make the
Analytics and Visualization template and the topics for these pre-built capabilities available in Agentforce
Studio.

Semantic Foundations for Agentforce in Tableau Next
Build a strong foundation for Agentforce for Analytics success with well-structured, well-designed
semantic models.
Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next
Concierge is a deeply integrated, intelligent layer for conversational analytics built to meet business
users where they work, across Tableau Next, Salesforce, and Slack.
Semantic Foundations for Agentforce in Tableau Next
Build a strong foundation for Agentforce for Analytics success with well-structured, well-designed
semantic models.
Design Semantic Models for AI Readiness
A well-designed semantic model is the key to getting accurate and relevant insights with agentic
analytics in Tableau Next. It helps your analytics agents understand the data and confidently answer
questions. When designing your semantic model, build on a foundation of AI readiness standards and
well-established industry best practices for data modeling and data quality.
Build an AI-Ready Semantic Model
A well-designed semantic model helps analytics agents understand data and confidently answer
questions. You'll need to make your semantic model AI-ready to provide real-world context and reduce
70

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
ambiguity for your agents. Success with using Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next requires the
agent to be grounded with an AI-ready semantic model.
Optimize and Test a Semantic Model
Building, enriching, and testing a semantic model is an iterative process rather than a linear one. As
you define and refine the semantic model, you can use various feedback mechanisms available in the
Semantic Model Builder to make incremental adjustments. As you fine-tune your approach, each
improvement can lead to better operational outcomes.
Design Semantic Models for AI Readiness
A well-designed semantic model is the key to getting accurate and relevant insights with agentic analytics
in Tableau Next. It helps your analytics agents understand the data and confidently answer questions.
When designing your semantic model, build on a foundation of AI readiness standards and well-
established industry best practices for data modeling and data quality.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Semantic Model Features
To support agentic analytics across Tableau Next and Salesforce 360, a semantic model must contain
Data Model Objects (DMOs) and can contain Data Lake Objects (DLOs). A semantic model can be based
on a Tableau Cloud PDS.
The semantic model must contain a complete and accurate relationship map of your data. Calculated
Insights (CIs) aren't currently supported.
Note You can create a semantic model in Data 360 by connecting it directly to a Published Data
Source (PDS) in Tableau. The PDS serves as the external data definition and query source for the
semantic model. This does not create DMOs in Data 360 and additional objects can't be added to
the model. For more information, see Connect a Published Data Source (PDS) to a New Semantic
Model.
Semantic Model AI Readiness
Building AI-ready semantic models is vital for user trust, agent performance, and business agility. By
following these best practices, you not only future-proof your data models — you also accelerate your
journey to scalable, agent-driven analytics.
Define clear object and field names
Agents interpret fields in the context of the objects they belong to. The clarity and specificity of object
71

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
and field names and labels play a crucial role in AI interpretability.
The API names (the system name, defined at creation in Data Cloud) and labels (the display name) of
objects and fields are used by AI agents.
The combination of object context and field properties drives semantic clarity. While generic labels like
"ID" or "Name" may seem ambiguous in isolation, they're acceptable if the object they belong to is well-
named and clearly described.
The API name can't be edited after creation in the semantic model, so it should be created thoughtfully.
The label can and should be tailored for business clarity.
UUnniiqquuee nnaammee ccoommbbiinnaattiioonnss
Ensure the combination of object name, description, and field label is sufficient for uniquely identifying
the field's role and purpose. Avoid abbreviations.
MMeeaanniinnggffuull,, rreelleevvaanntt llaabbeellss
Use meaningful, business-relevant display names, especially for custom fields. For example, pair an
object named "Customer" with a field labeled "ID" — this is more interpretable than an ambiguous
pairing like "Entity" with "ID".
DDeessccrriippttiivvee AAPPII nnaammeess
When creating new fields in Data Cloud, choose API names that are as descriptive as possible to improve
downstream interpretability.
Provide informative, balanced descriptions
Descriptions play a pivotal role in helping agents interpret fields and objects accurately.
SSttaannddaarrdd oobbjjeeccttss aanndd fifieellddss
For Salesforce Standard Objects, leaving the default description may be acceptable, as these are widely
known and referenced across public knowledge sources that agents are trained on. If a user modifies the
description of a standard object or field, it must be additive and not contradict existing knowledge.
UUssee SSaalleessffoorrccee ssttaannddaarrdd oobbjjeeccttss aanndd fifieellddss
Use Salesforce Standard Objects and Fields when designing your data model. Agents are more likely to
understand and interpret standard components correctly because they are well-documented more and
are used in public training data.
72

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
CCuussttoomm oobbjjeeccttss aanndd fifieellddss
For custom objects and fields, a clear and informative description is essential. It should reflect what the
data represents, align with industry standards whenever possible, and explain any unique internal
meanings when necessary.
CCrraafftt cclleeaarr ddeessccrriippttiioonnss
Provide a concise, 1–2 sentence description that:
•
Conveys the business context. For example, Purchase Date represents the Date field for
when the invoice was printed or sent to the user. In some cases the Transaction
Date and Purchase Date are different due to that reason.
•
Describes how it's being used in the business. For example, Purchase Date is the default Date
filter and slicer in most Purchasing and Costing related analysis.
•
Represents the data well. Review the data, and make sure the description doesn’t only describe the
general purpose of the field. It should elaborate if there are specific data values with a specific
purpose. For example, 'NA' represents transactions in which their purchase wasn't
confirmed yet and aligns with Transaction Status = 'Pending'.
Test the new description against agent understanding by asking your favorite chat tool “How do you
interpret this description content for the field field name?”
SSeemmaannttiicc mmooddeellss
Agents incorporate the description of a semantic model. Descriptions that include use cases and goals
for the model are vital.
DDeessccrriibbee mmooddeell ccoonntteexxtt
Provide a clear description of the semantic model that conveys its goals and use cases. It provides
essential context about the model's purpose and scope, and improves the agent’s ability to reason about
its content.
Structure the model for agent confidence
A well-connected and well-organized semantic model helps agents interpret queries correctly and
answer questions accurately.
Island tables or isolated objects (tables and objects with limited or no relationships with other tables and
objects) are sometimes intentionally created within a semantic model for distinct use cases. These
disconnected structures can cause confusion. Both agents and users may misinterpret these structures or
try to query them as if they are connected, even though relationships are not defined.
73

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
CCoonnnneecctt mmooddeell ccoonntteennttss
Make sure all objects are part of a single connected cluster. Avoid ambiguous or unjoined objects that
could trigger invalid responses from agents.
Detect and resolve ambiguities
Agents commonly analyze the full component properties when interpreting semantic models. However,
it's essential to ensure that any ambiguity can be resolved solely based on the label and description level.
Ambiguity can arise from the existence of similar entities or objects, similar fields, calculated fields, and
metrics — and sometimes even between calculated fields and regular fields.
Agentforce for Analytics evaluates the complete set of object properties—including Object Name, Object
Description, Field Display Name, Field Descriptions, Field Data Type, Field Role, Sample Values, and
object/field relationships—to reduce ambiguity. However, because it's common for multiple fields to carry
similar data or serve overlapping purposes, the semantic model must clearly differentiate between them
through precise and intentional metadata.
RReegguullaarrllyy rruunn ssiimmiillaarriittyy aanndd aammbbiigguuiittyy cchheecckkss
Ensure that each component created and added to the semantic model serves a unique purpose, and
that this purpose is clearly captured in both the label and description.
Note The Semantic Model AI Optimization tool includes a Similarity scan that can address most of
the scenarios described here. Use this tool in the Semantic Model Builder to quickly scan the model
and reduce similarities.
CCoommmmoonn ssoouurrcceess ooff aammbbiigguuiittyy
•
Overlapping descriptions – Vague or identical field descriptions that fail to clarify distinct roles or
usage. Since it's common to copy and paste descriptions, often it leads to ambiguity.
•
Similar business context – Fields that represent the same concept but appear in different tables (for
example “Customer Region” from Orders Header vs. Order Line).
Note You can define business-specific knowledge in your semantic model using Business
Preferences. This helps the agent respond to analytical questions within your business's unique
context and logic.
•
Synonyms or homonyms – Fields with different names representing the same concept (synonyms), or
fields with identical names representing different concepts (homonyms).
•
Objects which exist twice for different purposes and uses, but it's not clear from their semantics that
they differ. Here are some common examples:
- Same object, different purpose – A generic Date field in the model, which is being used with both
Purchase Date and Transaction Date.
- Same object, different granularity – A detailed Order Lines object used for drill down to the order
level when needed, and an aggregated version of Order Lines created for performance optimization.
74

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
- Constraint workarounds – An object exists more than once in the model with differing contents to
get around a limitation or constraint of a source model. For example, a user extended a base model
that includes an Account object with only five of the fields they need. Because they can’t adjust the
base model, an additional Account object was added with all columns (existing and new). This may
also be encountered in models with Published Data Sources or logical views.
Manage similar calculated fields and metrics
Agents frequently analyze formula definitions directly, which can introduce confusion even when labels
and descriptions are clear. Calculated fields with similar or overlapping logic can lead to
misinterpretation of the semantic model. Similarly, metrics that differ only by minor filter variations can
create comparable confusion. These cases should be managed with the same rigor applied to calculated
fields.
AAvvooiidd hhaavviinngg ssiimmiillaarr ccaallccuullaattiioonnss
Hide or remove calculated fields and metrics that don't deliver a clear, distinct analytical purpose. If
removal isn't feasible, clearly define their role and differentiate them explicitly in their label and
description to avoid ambiguity at the formula level.
Enrich Semantic Models with Focused Semantic Components
The semantic layer should represent business logic accurately and also anticipate how agents will
consume and reason over it. Agents are aware of the relative semantic strength of different model
components. Curated metrics and calculated logic carry higher intent and clarity. As a result, agents
prioritize interpreting and using metrics first, followed by calculated fields, and lastly, raw fields.
Metrics as a semantic anchor
A metric is a high-value semantic object designed to answer specific business questions with clarity.
A metric includes
•
Targeted calculations
•
Time-scoped logic
•
Contextual filtering
•
Business-consistent formatting
DDeefifinnee mmeettrriiccss ffoorr iimmppoorrttaanntt ccaallccuullaattiioonnss
Define a metric for each high-impact and highly used calculated field or field measure. This guides
agents in understanding where and when specific logic applies.
75

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
Predefined calculated fields
Calculated fields should encapsulate business logic that agents would otherwise need to infer. Defining
them in advance improves answer quality, reduces ambiguity, and boosts trust.
UUssee ccaallccuullaatteedd fifieellddss jjuuddiicciioouussllyy
Only include calculated fields with clear analytical purpose. Avoid overloading the model with rarely-used
or overly niche calculations.
Explicit field types
Classifying fields as measures or dimensions at the metadata level enhances agent reasoning. It signals
how data should behave in analytical workflows, for example, as an aggregate instead of a filter.
AAssssiiggnn sseemmaannttiicc rroolleess
Add a semantic role as field type to each field. These cues help agents apply them correctly in queries.
VVaalliiddaattee fifieelldd mmaappppiinnggss
Validate that fields are mapped to the correct type for an optimized agentic experience. For example, for
a date field, use a field type of date instead of text. This helps the agent to interpret the date field
correctly.

known meaning.

Review calculated fields and regular fields with similar logic for overlap.

Build an AI-Ready Semantic Model
A well-designed semantic model helps analytics agents understand data and confidently answer
questions. You'll need to make your semantic model AI-ready to provide real-world context and reduce
ambiguity for your agents. Success with using Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next requires the
agent to be grounded with an AI-ready semantic model.

11.. Understand AI-readiness in semantic models
Building the semantic model requires following best practices as you design and define it. Tableau
Next also provides tools to help you make the semantic model ready for use with the analytics agents
and Concierge.
22.. Select a semantic model
Create a semantic model or use one that already exists. Add data model objects (DMOs) and define
relationships. You can do this manually, or with AI-assistance using Einstein or Data Pro in the
Semantic Model Builder.
33.. Create calculated fields
If needed, create calculated fields. You can do this manually, or with AI-assistance using Einstein or
Data Pro in the Semantic Model Builder. For example, you might create calculated fields for KPIs of
interest or for dimension fields that don't physically exist in your data set.
77

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
44.. Add descriptions
Add descriptions to reduce ambiguity in objects and calculated fields. You can do this manually, or use
AI-generated descriptions.
55.. Define business preferences
Add business preferences to define business-specific knowledge within the semantic model. You will
want to provide tailored business preferences for unique jargon, logic, and concepts in your domain.
You can revisit this step during optimization as you test and refine the model more.
66.. Define metrics
Define metrics in the semantic model for high-impact and highly-used calculated fields or field
measures. Metrics contain targeted calculations, time-scoped logic, contextual filters, and formatting.
They provide context that guides agents in understanding where and when specific logic applies.
77.. Enable analytics agent readiness
Enable the semantic model for use with an analytics agent created from the Analytics and
Visualization template.
88.. Validate model
Validate and test the model in the Semantic Model Builder to make sure that it’s complete: all required
DMOs, relationships, and calculated fields are included. Also make sure metrics and calculated fields
aren't duplicated in the model.

Use Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta) to gain clear visibility into your semantic model's health and
AI-readiness, and see suggested recommendations to improve it. This tool runs a series of automated
checks against your semantic model and calculates an overall model health for AI-readiness.
Use Q&A Calibration (Beta) in Tableau Next to improve accuracy in Concierge. Test questions in
Concierge and validate the agent’s answers against your specific data and rules. The list of Verified
questions that you compile and save improves agent accuracy for similar questions. When you mark a
question as Inaccurate, you can further fine-tune business preferences, update fields and metadata, and
update calculations to improve accuracy and performance. After you make the changes, you can retest
the questions until they are accurate.
Note After using Q&A Calibration, you can also use manual testing to determine if the model is
complete and generates the queries you are expecting.

Manually test large sets of questions
Use Q&A Calibration to save Verified questions
Q&A Calibration (Beta) is a good starting point for testing basic questions in Concierge. Use Q&A
Calibration to test, classify, and calibrate questions. The list of verified questions that you compile and
save improves agent accuracy for similar questions.
11.. Collect sample questions
Gather questions from business users, use your own experience, or have an LLM generate a list of likely
82

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
queries.
22.. Test and analyze
As you test questions using Concierge in Q&A Calibration, analyze the agent's performance including
its query logic and the accuracy of responses for your organization's needs. Evaluate whether the
responses meet your user's needs and match how they talk about the business and data in your
organization.
33.. Classify
Classify responses as Verified or Inaccurate. When a response is inaccurate, investigate why. What
query did it use? What parts of the query are wrong?
44.. Calibrate
Make incremental changes in the semantic model. Calibrate the semantic model by updating business
preferences, fields, and calculations.
55.. Retest and refine
Ask the question again in Concierge. Observe the impact of these incremental changes on the
accuracy of the responses in Concierge.
Use manual testing for large sets of questions
When you have a large number of questions to be tested, test the questions directly in Concierge in a
supported Tableau Next surface. Use a spreadsheet to record each question and the response results.
Improve accuracy through a continuous cycle of testing and refinement.
11.. Collect sample questions
What do your business users want to ask? Gather questions from your users, use your own experience,
or use an LLM to generate a list of likely queries.
22.. Test and analyze
Use those initial questions as a guide in developing your semantic model. Record the questions, the
responses, inaccuracies, and ideas for improvements in a spreadsheet.
Test your sample questions in Concierge against a semantic model in Tableau Next. Observe where
the agent response is incorrect, and then try to figure out where the agent has gaps and where you
may need to enhance the model.
Analyze if the model is complete. Is it generating the queries you are expecting? Open the
Troubleshooting Information details in Concierge to review the query logic. What parts of the query
are incorrect?
33.. Diagnose
83

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
Hypothesize why the response or logic might be inaccurate. What changes could you make to the
semantic model to improve the response? Identify missing or incorrect data, missing or incorrect
fields, missing calculations and dimensions, and incorrect terms during this phase. Could adding
business preferences or descriptions improve the response?
44.. Refine and retest
Continuously refine the model based on testing insights before rolling it out to a pilot user group for
ongoing feedback. Make the changes you think will help and retest the same questions. Record
responses and determine if accuracy improved or more refinement is needed. Continue this process
until the response is accurate for your list of questions.
Note As you discover commonly asked questions with accurate responses, consider add these
questions in Q&A Calibration and mark them as Verified. This helps the agent learn and respond to
similar questions more accurately, in language that is tailored to your organization.
Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next
Concierge is a deeply integrated, intelligent layer for conversational analytics built to meet business users
where they work, across Tableau Next, Salesforce, and Slack.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
When Agentforce for Analytics and Concierge: Analytics Q&A are enabled in Tableau Next, you can
explore your metric details and dashboards through natural conversation in the Agentforce panel. Ask
questions about your data to get answers in the form of insights and relevant visualizations. For
transparency, responses from the agent include the sources and reasoning used for analysis.
When Agentforce isn't enabled, the metric details page uses the default insight exploration experience
without the use of generative AI.
Note Analysts and Administrators: For information on how to plan, enable, and implement
Concierge, see Plan Your Concierge Implementation in Tableau Next.
Concierge: Analytics Q&A is available through the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce. This
template uses the pre-built Data Analysis subagent with the default Analyze Data action to craft rich-text,
natural language responses in Tableau Next that directly answer your questions about data.
Concierge requires that any semantic model it queries is enabled for use with Agentforce for Analytics in
Tableau Next. A Tableau Next user who has Semantic Model editing permission must enable the
semantic model in the Analytics Agent Readiness dialog box. For more information, see Enable Analytics
Agent Readiness in Data 360 help.
84

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
Note Admins can create fallback lists of semantic models per agent for scenarios where the
semantic context isn't clear. For more information, see Set Up Agent Scoping for Concierge.
Concierge: Analytics Q&A conversationally:
•
Answers questions in a session using the underlying data from the semantic model as a grounded
source to query against and analyze.
•
Clarifies intent when it encounters complexity or ambiguity in a question to provide a better, more
accurate response.
Concierge uses a single semantic query path for questions.
Semantic queries interact with the underlying semantic model dynamically, allowing for custom
breakdowns, entity comparisons, and deeper exploration. Concierge: Analytics Q&A generates and runs a
semantic query against the underlying semantic model of an associated metric or dashboard. It
synthesizes the query output data to create a textual answer and creates a visualization based on the
semantic query.
When Agentforce for Analytics is enabled in Slack, you can use it to ask questions about shared Tableau
Next metrics and dashboard data in that context. For more information, see Ask Questions About Shared
Metrics and Dashboards in Slack Using Agentforce for Analytics.
Note Concierge: Analytics Q&A and the Agentforce platform are built on top of the Einstein Trust
Layer and inherit all of its security, governance, and trust capabilities. As you and other users interact
with the analytics agent created for your Tableau Next org, no data or conversations are saved to the
Large Language Model (LLM), and no customer data is ever used to train the model.Trust is built
into the experience by inviting thumbs-up or thumbs-down ratings and open feedback. These
responses are used to improve the conversational experience. For more information, see Trust and
Agentforce..

includes five major phases.

questions to explore your data in the Agentforce panel (in a metric details page, dashboard view, or
embedded Tableau Next dashboard). When Agentforce for Analytics is enabled in Slack, you can use it
to ask questions about shared Tableau Next metric and dashboard data in that context.

Salesforce, and Slack. This topic lists the types of questions, surfaces, and contexts where Concierge is
currently supported.
