Plan Your Concierge Implementation in Tableau Next
As an analyst implementing Concierge in Tableau Next, you play an integral role in getting Concierge to
work well for your organization. Successfully enabling Concierge: Analytics Q&A for use in Tableau Next
includes five major phases.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Phase 1 Define how Concierge can help you do your work
Phase 2 Set up Agentforce for Analytics, Concierge, and Data Pro in Tableau Next
Phase 3 Make your semantic model AI-ready to provide context for Concierge
Phase 4 Optimize, test, and calibrate the semantic model to work with Concierge
Phase 5 Progressively roll out Concierge to your organization
Define how Concierge can help you do your work
Think of your day-to-day experience as an analyst. What inquiries about data do you find yourself
answering frequently? What questions and answers do you wish Concierge could handle for you?
As the analyst, you're the expert on your data, your business, and the types of questions an analytics
agent must answer. Think of Concierge as a junior analyst who you're mentoring. The time you put into
gathering use cases, getting the right data, enriching your semantic models, and providing context for
Concierge will make this analytics agent a highly valuable asset that ultimately ends up saving you time in
the future.
Before you turn it on, consider the best use for Concierge in your business. Concierge is highly effective
at exploring and answering "what" and "why" questions about data. It can answer the most commonly
86

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
asked data questions and save time for your analysts.
11.. Identify common analytics questions for Concierge. Concierge is best suited to answer general
analytical questions about your data.
••
Trends: Questions about growth or changes over time, often broken down by region, portfolio, or
product line.
••
Rankings and KPIs: Queries to rank performance, such as identifying the best-performing
campaigns or sales representatives.
••
Comparisons: Analyzes how different factors, like products or accounts, contribute to deals
compared to previous periods.
••
Breakdowns: Explores how the total is broken down by different categories.
••
Follow-up Reasoning: Ask a series of sub-questions to drill deeper into an analysis.
22.. Collect and create a list of example test questions (utterances) for Concierge. What are common
business questions your organization must be able to ask about data? Gather questions from business
users, use your own experience, or have an LLM generate a list of likely queries.
33.. Determine the data you need for the questions and use cases you have chosen. This process includes
making sure you are connected to the data you need in Data 360. The data may require some
transformation, either in the source data (for example, Snowflake) or in Data 360.
Set up Agentforce for Analytics, Concierge, and Data Pro in Tableau Next
In this phase, you'll configure users, turn on organization features, and create and activate an agent for
analytics and visualizations in Tableau Next.
11.. Configure Tableau Next for Agentforce and AI. This step includes enabling Tableau Next, Einstein, and
Agentforce and assigning user permission sets.
22.. Turn on settings for agentic analytics in Tableau Next. These settings make the Analytics and
Visualization agent template and its topics and actions available in Agentforce Studio.
Optionally turn on settings to support building an AI-ready semantic model.
33.. Create an agent with the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce Studio.
For detailed steps on this phase, see Set Up Agentforce for Analytics.
Make your semantic model AI-ready to provide context for Concierge
In this phase, you'll follow best practices and use tools available in the Semantic Model Builder to get the
semantic model ready for use with an analytics agent.
Your goal in designing the semantic model is to provide business context for the agent, and to make it
easy to explain questions, answers, and the semantic model without assistance. If a human analyst who
is new to the semantic model can understand it without assistance, Concierge will likely be able to do
the same.
87

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
11.. Become familiar with the concepts in Design a Semantic Model for AI-Readiness. This topic describes
best practices to consider when building a semantic model.
22.. If you haven't already, add and build a semantic model in Tableau Next. This includes adding data
model objects (DMOs) and relationships, either manually or with AI assistance. If needed, create
calculated fields. You can do this manually, or with AI assistance using Einstein or Data Pro in the
Semantic Model Builder.
33.. Define metrics in the semantic model for high-impact KPIs and highly-used calculated fields or field
measures. Also, review any visualizations, dashboards, and metrics you have created in Tableau Next
based on the semantic model.
44.. Add descriptions to reduce ambiguity in objects and calculated fields. You can do this manually, or
using AI-generated descriptions.
55.. Add business preferences to define business-specific knowledge within the semantic model.
66.. Enable the semantic model for use with an analytics agent created from the Analytics and
Visualization template. This step is required to be able use the analytics agent.
Note Admins can create fallback lists of semantic models per agent for scenarios where the
semantic context isn't clear. For more information, see Set Up Agent Scoping for Concierge.
For detailed steps on this phase, see Build an AI-Ready Semantic Model.
Optimize, test, and calibrate the semantic model
Implementation is an iterative process rather than a linear one. During every step of defining and refining
the semantic model, use the feedback available in the Semantic Model Builder to check your results,
review your assumptions, ask new questions, make adjustments, and try again. As you fine-tune your
approach, each improvement can help lead to better accuracy and responses.
The Semantic Model Builder can offer suggestions for optimizing different parts of the model. Manual
testing helps you determine if the model is complete and generates the queries you are expecting. Q&A
Calibration makes it easier to test questions in Concierge and create a list of verified questions to
improve agent accuracy.
During this phase, optimize, test, and refine the semantic model to recognize questions and provide
meaningful answers about the data in Concierge. The goal of this process is to improve the accuracy of
Concierge responses through a continuous cycle of testing and refinement.
•
Use Semantic AI Model Optimization (Beta) suggestions to improve semantic model health. This tool
runs a series of automated checks against your semantic model and calculates an overall model health
for AI-readiness.
•
Use Q&A Calibration (Beta) to test questions in Concierge and classify responses as Verified or
Inaccurate. Where a response is inaccurate, investigate the reasons suggested by Q&A Calibration. You
can also review the query logic used for the response. What query did it use? What parts of the query
are wrong? Next, you can calibrate your model using the suggested calibrations, tailored to your
questions. Calibrations include adding or updating business preferences, fields, and calculations.
Note You must have analysts and administrator permissions to use Semantic AI Model Optimization
88

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
and Q&A Calibration.
As you make changes to the semantic model, test questions again in Concierge and continue testing and
updating until you get the responses you expect.
For detailed steps on this phase, see Optimize and Test the Semantic Model.
Progressively roll out Concierge in your organization
When you are done with the testing phase, start progressively rolling out Concierge in your organization
to learn more about the actual questions that people ask—not just what you thought they'd ask.
When you are done with the testing phase, start progressively rolling out Concierge in your organization
to learn more about the actual questions that people ask—not just what you thought they'd ask.
11.. Start with 2-5 coworkers who enjoy testing analytics software solutions in your organization. For
example, start with your analyst colleagues. Note what questions they ask in Concierge and how it
answers. Are the answers Concierge provides useful?
22.. Expand your set of test questions over time as you see the types of questions real users are asking.
As you continue to test and get improved results, try a pilot with 20-50 people in your organization.
Continue to monitor the questions, and test and calibrate the semantic model.
Add common questions with accurate responses to Q&A Calibration and mark them as Verified. This
helps the agent learn and respond to similar questions more accurately, in language that is tailored to
your organization.
33.. When you feel confident about how Concierge is performing, try rolling it out to an entire group or
department, and then the whole organization.
As you roll out Concierge, give your users guidance and context to help them be successful when
asking questions. Educate them about what kinds of questions they can ask and what types of
questions aren't supported. For more information, see Ask Questions with Concierge and Supported
Questions and Surfaces for Concierge.

embedded Tableau Next dashboard). When Agentforce for Analytics is enabled in Slack, you can use it to
ask questions about shared Tableau Next metric and dashboard data in that context.

Concierge: Analytics Q&A Limitations
In Tableau Next, Agentforce for Analytics has the following limitations related to questions and
responses in Concierge.
Limitations on Questions in Analytics Q&A
Limitation Description
Organization Hierarchy Scope Concierge can analyze questions that use "my",
but it can't resolve questions that refer to
organizational hierarchy such as "my team", "my
department", "my employees", "my manager"
92

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
Limitation Description
Multiple Query Questions Questions that require two or more queries aren't
currently supported.
Forecasting or Predictive Questions Questions related to predictions or forecasting
aren't currently supported. For example, "Predict
sales for the next 3 quarters"
Hypothesis Testing Questions related to hypothesis testing aren't
currently supported. For example, "How will my
ACV change if I reduce churn in Enterprise
accounts by 5%"
Root Cause Analysis Questions related to root cause analysis aren't
currently supported. For example, "Whats causing
my churn rate to increase?"
Window functions other than Period over Period Questions about SQL window functions other
than Period over Period aren't currently
supported. For example, "What's the running
average?"
Questions about ordered set aggregations
Ordered set aggregations:
(Median, Rank) aren't currently supported. For
example, "How is my salary ranked amongst my
Median and Rank
colleagues" or "What is the median salary?"
Custom Fiscal Calendar Questions about customer fiscal calendar settings
aren't currently supported. For example, a
reference to a "13 month year"
Dashboard Selection Context Actions in a dashboard are not considered in
agent responses.
Context for Questions in Slack When Agentforce for Analytics is enabled in Slack,
users must click the Agentforce button in an
embedded metric or dashboard in Slack to ask
questions.
Supported Visualizations for Responses Concierge supports bar charts, line charts, and
table charts in responses to questions.
Advanced Analytics Questions on correlation, anomaly detection, or
statistical analysis aren't currently supported.
Tips for Improving Performance in Concierge: Analytics Q&A
Multiple factors can affect performance in Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next. This topic lists tips
93

Tableau Next Agentforce for Analytics
for improving query performance when asking questions in Concierge.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
For a complete overview of the phases required to implement Concierge, see, Plan Your Concierge
Implementation in Tableau Next.
Prepare the dataset intentionally
•
Design joins carefully. Avoid many-to-many or non-strict joins (such as BETWEEN) that expand result
sets and slow queries. Example: If joining Opportunities to Accounts, ensure join keys are unique
(such as AccountID) to prevent result explosion.
•
Push complexity into ETL. Heavy calculations or derived fields (such as Forecasted Revenue) should be
pre-computed in Data Cloud transforms, not recalculated at runtime.
Example: Instead of computing forecasted opportunity close rate in runtime, compute it in ETL and
expose it as a metric.
•
Handle large fact tables (>100 million rows) thoughtfully. Pre-compute aggregations (for example,
monthly revenue totals) or apply semantic model filters to reduce data scanned at runtime.
Example: For a Sales fact table with 500 million rows, pre-aggregate ACV by quarter to help Concierge
answer "Show me ACV by quarter" without scanning the full dataset.
Follow best practices for semantic model design
•
Include only objects and fields needed for the business.
•
Streamlined semantic models ensure faster query generation and improve the accuracy of field
mappings.
For in-depth guidelines for designing and refining semantic models in Tableau Next, see Design Semantic
Models for AI Readiness and Build an AI-Ready Semantic Model.
Pre-define metrics
Define metrics in the semantic model that directly support frequently asked utterances.
•
Metrics provide context that can help guide analytics agents for understanding where and when
specific logic applies.
•
Defining metrics improves latency by avoiding expensive ad hoc query paths and ensures consistency
across your org.
Example: "What is my ACV?" creates the ACV metric once in the semantic model. Future utterances
referencing "ACV" benefit from faster routing and consistent logic.
Ask targeted questions
Ask targeted questions rather than open-ended questions in Concierge. Targeted questions reduce the
amount of data that must be analyzed, improve query latency, and prevent timeouts on very large
datasets.
94

Tableau Next Data Governance and Sharing In Tableau Next
•
Limit the scope of your questions (utterances) to reduce the query scope.
•
Ask questions that clearly specify time ranges. Example: "Show me sales for Q1 2025" vs. "Show me
sales"
•
Simplify questions to include fewer categories, measures, or aggregations.
•
Avoid asking for long lists of records. Ask questions that don't require thousands of records to reduce
the query scope. Example: "What are the top 10 opportunities?" vs. "Show me all opportunities?"

Data Visibility - Policies
Policies allow and deny access to Data 360 objects (like DLOs, DMOs, and CIs) and records and also
mask fields within data spaces. They can be widely applied with minimal data preparation and are
98

Tableau Next Data Governance and Sharing In Tableau Next
implemented differently than security in Tableau or CRM Analytics.
In Tableau, data security is generally managed with record-level security inherited from the data source,
either through a manually-defined entitlement table or by pass-through authentication.
In CRM Analytics, data security is defined independently on each dataset, either through sharing
inheritance or a security predicate driven by augments, calculated fields, and hierarchy flattening in data
prep.
A Data 360 administrator can define policies in Data Governance in Data 360.
Data 360 Policies in Tableau Next
Data 360 controls data access centrally, through policies defined in Data Governance. In contrast, CRM
Analytics defines data access at the dataset level and record-level security in Tableau is driven by the data
source.
OObbjjeecctt--lleevveell aanndd rreeccoorrdd--lleevveell ppoolliicciieess
Record-level security (RLS) is more restrictive than object-level security (OLS). If any RLS is defined on an
object, all access to that object will require explicitly defined RLS policies.
PPoolliiccyy eevvaalluuaattiioonn
Rather than the binary “true or false” predicate evaluation in CRM Analytics or an entitlement table in
Tableau, Data 360 policies are evaluated in layers.
Deny
Policies that deny access to data are applied first. Restrict data access to records and fields with
classifications, tags, and user attributes. Masking and allow policies can’t override a deny policy.
Note Users with View All Records or Modify All Records permission A user with one of these
powerful permissions can see object and field metadata, even if a deny policy restricts their access
to the data in that object or field. This allows them to interact with the objects and fields in
semantic models, visualizations, metrics, and other places without seeing the contents.
Mask
Policies that mask fields are applied next. A masking policy obfuscates the value in a specific
field. Masking policies can’t override a deny policy.
Allow
Policies that allow access are applied last. Grant data access to objects and records with
classifications, tags, and user attributes, including hierarchies. Allow policies can’t override a
deny or masking policy.
99

Tableau Next Data Governance and Sharing In Tableau Next
MMeettaaddaattaa ppoolliiccyy ccrriitteerriiaa
Data 360 uses metadata in the form of Data Tags and Classifications as criteria for policies. This allows for
flexible, scalable security such as “Deny access to all fields tagged as PII.” Data governance also supports
logic using explicit field value comparisons, similar to CRM Analytics and Tableau security.
DDaattaa ssttrruuccttuurree ccoonnssiiddeerraattiioonnss
Data 360 can evaluate policies against separate tables and data sources with joins built into policy
criteria. CRM Analytics requires that data used for security predicate evaluation is present in the dataset.
When designing your governance plan for Tableau Next, think of policy criteria (including metadata and
user attributes, hierarchies, and entitlements) as the record-level filters applied to the data, rather than
being part of the data that is being filtered. No need to determine grain, flatten multi-layer relationship
tables, and build complex join and augment structures in data prep.

Data Tagging and Classification in Data 360
Data Source Record-Level Security
Configure record-level security differently depending on the data source.
DDaattaa 336600 DDMMOO oorr DDLLOO
Define policies in Data 360 to control record-level access.
ZZeerroo CCooppyy DDLLOO
Data source connections return records based on the access of the authentication user defined in the
connection. The DLO doesn’t apply security based on the logged-in user by default. To control record-
level access, define policies based on user attributes in Data 360.
If you’ve already configured an entitlement table for your external data source, don’t despair! You can
define record-level security with an entitlement table.
TTaabblleeaauu PPuubblliisshheedd DDaattaa SSoouurrccee ((PPDDSS))
A PDS is a direct connection, it isn’t represented as a DLO in Data 360. Data access isn’t configured
through data governance. For users configured in Tableau Cloud, record-level security is inherited from
the PDS.
Allow Policy Patterns in Tableau Next
Policy criteria can be layered and mixed to achieve your desired data visibility results. Here are some
100

Tableau Next Data Governance and Sharing In Tableau Next
common policy structures.
Define an allow policy with the following selections in Data Governance.
•
Resource: Record
•
Action: Allow access
•
Apply To Object: Select object...
Choose when the policy’s action (in this case, allowing access) should occur.
Criteria within a policy can be linked together in logical groups to build complex access patterns. Add a
group to Take Action On Records which meet any condition (OR) for a set of criteria that each
grants access, or Records which meet all conditions (AND) for a set of criteria that all must be
true to grant access.
RReeccoorrdd oowwnneerrsshhiipp
Data 360 doesn’t have automatic record owner visibility. If a DLO or DMO has a field containing the
record owner’s Salesforce User Id, this condition tests if the user is the record’s owner.
Field Name Object_Name > Owner Id Is Equal To User > User Id
Note This is equivalent to 'ObjectOwnerId' == "$User.Id" in a CRM Analytics security
predicate.This is similar to using the USERNAME() fuction to filter extracted rows in Tableau.Record
ownership must be included at the root DMO or DLO level. Filtering on user data joined to an object
in the semantic layer shouldn’t be used in place of a security feature.
WWoorrkkiinngg wwiitthh SSaalleessffoorrccee hhiieerraarrcchhiieess
Before role, manager, or territory hierarchies in Salesforce can be used in policies, they need to be
connected to a Data 360 data source. Set up a Salesforce CRM Connector to the org where the target
hierarchy resides then add a new hierarchy with the steps in Ingest Hierarchical Data.
RRoollee hhiieerraarrcchhyy
After ingesting the Salesforce role hierarchy, this condition tests if the user is hierarchically above the
record’s owner.
Field Name Object_Name > Owner Is Hierarchally Above In Role_Hierarchy > User
Note This is equivalent to 'ObjectOwner.Roles.Roles' == "$User.UserRoleId" in a CRM
Analytics security predicate.

RLS with Entitlement Tables
To control record-level security for zero-copy DLOs, connect an entitlement table that describes user-level
record access and define an allow policy with it. Upload a CSV file and create a filtered join in the policy.

Asset Access - Sharing
Sharing an asset within Tableau Next grants another user visibility and capabilities related to that asset.
Sharing doesn’t override policy-based restrictions.
An asset owner can Manage Access to Assets in Tableau Next. Users with the Tableau Unmetered Admin
or Tableau Next Admin permission set can manage sharing for all Tableau Next assets.

Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
The Tableau Next analytics home page offers a quick way to build analytics assets, organize them within
workspaces and access the resources and assets available to you.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
After enabling Tableau Next for your organization, you can open it from the App Launcher.
Create workspaces and other assets in just a few clicks (1). Review a curated list of resources, such as
videos and Trailhead modules, to familiarize yourself with Tableau Next (2). Resume working on the
assets you, as the logged-in user, opened most recently from the top of the home page (3). The Explore
section lists assets based on these filters: workspace, user, and asset type (4). You can also search for
your assets by entering a keyword (5).
To take action on an asset quickly, use its context menu. For example, to view an asset, click Open.

A workspace is a flexible place for analysts to create, add, and reuse analytic assets to answer
questions about their data.

Tableau Next Workspaces
A workspace is a flexible place for analysts to create, add, and reuse analytic assets to answer questions
about their data.
103

Tableau Next Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Each workspace contains the tools needed for data analysis, including data connections, a semantic
model builder and metric creator, and visualization tools to share findings with others.
You can create or add existing assets to associate them with a workspace, including:
•
Connections to data model objects (DMOs) and data lake objects (DLOs) in Data Cloud
•
Semantic models, either reused from Data Cloud or built in your workspace
•
Data visualizations
•
Dashboards
•
Existing calculated insights objects (CIOs)
•
Uploaded .csv data
Workspace assets can be used to build more analytical assets or reused in other workspaces.
Note Because a workspace represents a unit of work, you can have as many workspaces in Tableau
Next as you need to organize your team’s work and answer key business questions with data.
Analyze Data in Tableau Next Workspaces
Inside a workspace, assets are organized by type. The list view shows your available workspace assets
in real time. The layers of the workspace add context about the building blocks of workspace assets, so
an analyst has the information needed to answer questions with data.
Create a Workspace in Tableau Next
Everything in Tableau Next begins in a workspace. To create an analytical asset in Tableau Next, you
must start in a workspace.
Create or Add an Asset in a Workspace
Assets can be created within a workspace, or you can add an existing asset to your workspace or from
Data Data 360 if the administrator has made these assets visible.
Edit or Delete a Workspace in Tableau Next
Update a workspace name and description, or delete the workspace and its assets altogether. If the
workspace reused an asset from somewhere else, deleting the workspace won't affect the original
asset.

Analyze Data in Tableau Next Workspaces
Inside a workspace, assets are organized by type. The list view shows your available workspace assets in
real time. The layers of the workspace add context about the building blocks of workspace assets, so an
analyst has the information needed to answer questions with data.
104

Tableau Next Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
When reading through assets from the bottom to the top, each type of asset is a building block for the
next type of asset above it in the list. For example, you use data to create a semantic model, which you
can use to create a visualization. You can combine visualizations to create a dashboard for performing
analytics.
A workspace includes these asset types.
•
Data: Connections between your workspace and a data model object (DMO), data lake object (DLO)
or calculated insight object (CIO) in Data 360. A DMO is a grouping of data (made up of attributes)
created from data streams (a data source brought into Data 360), insights, and other sources. A CIO is
a type of DMO created after a calculated insight is processed. You can use data connections to build
semantic models.
•
Semantic models: Metadata in Data 360 that groups semantic definitions tailored for a specific
analytical use case. A semantic model consists of one or more data objects. You can use a semantic
model to build a visualization.
•
Visualizations: A visual representation of your data exploration that starts with a semantic model of
data. You can use visualizations to create dashboards in the same workspace.
•
Dashboards: A visual representation of multiple visualizations or other analytic assets for the purpose
of conveying different but related information.
To refine the list of assets appearing in your list view, use the Owner filter or search field.
105

Tableau Next Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
Workspace Assets and Dependencies
Changes to an asset also change the asset’s dependencies. For example, if you update a semantic model,
the visualizations that use that semantic model also change.
Likewise, you can create an asset while authoring another. For example, you could create a visualization
while authoring a dashboard, and both new assets (dashboard and visualization) are added to the
workspace.
You can only delete an asset in the workspace that it was created in. If the asset is reused from another
workspace, you can remove it from your workspace but not delete it. The original asset remains in the
source workspace as well as other workspaces that are reusing the asset.
Workspace Tabs
Each workspace or asset you open appears as a tab. You can work in multiple workspaces or on multiple
assets at one time. If you open an asset, it appears as a tab to the right of the workspace list tab.
Creating, adding, or editing an existing asset opens the asset in an additional new tab.
Create a Workspace in Tableau Next
Everything in Tableau Next begins in a workspace. To create an analytical asset in Tableau Next, you must
start in a workspace.

Next Personal Org (Beta)
A Personal Org is your own dedicated space designed primarily for self-service analysis and exploration.
108

Tableau Next Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
You’re able to freely combine corporate data with your personal datasets and create content without
affecting the production environment.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS

Note Tableau Next Personal Org is a pilot or beta service that is subject to the Beta Services Terms at
Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer, and

Build Your Analysis in the Personal Org
Your Personal Org gives you the autonomy to build and refine your work on your own terms. Once your
analysis is complete and ready for review, you can share it with analysts for feedback before it is
considered for broader consumption.
Feature Details
Combine Data Connect production data with data, such as a local
spreadsheet or a specialized dataset, to create new models
and visualizations. This process enables you to work with
both governed and individual data sources in a safe,
contained space.
Build and Explore All of your work remains private in your Personal Org. This
isolation enables you to freely test new ideas and make
revisions without affecting the governed content used by
others. You can develop your analysis iteratively, knowing that
your progress is secure and separate from production data,
models, and dashboards.
Work Autonomously By working in your Personal Org, you ensure that your in-
progress work doesn't disrupt the production environment.
This separation maintains the integrity of trusted data and
prevents unapproved content from being used.
Review Your Personal Org
Use the org picker (1) to confirm your current environment. Use search (2) to locate assets, and
recommendation cards (3) are available to guide your first steps. For quick access, recently opened and
modified assets (4) are listed at the bottom of the page.
109

Tableau Next Tableau Next Home and Workspaces
Request a Personal Org
To begin your independent data exploration and analysis, contact your Tableau Next admin to request a
personal org. After your personal org is ready, you will receive an email with access instructions.
Access Your Personal Org (Beta)
You can easily switch from your production environment to your Personal Org, even though it's a
separate environment, without needing to log in again.
Reuse Production Org Assets (Beta)
Your Personal Org provides the flexibility to integrate and experiment with data sources (DMOs),
visualizations, and semantic models to create comprehensive views for analysis.
Sharing Assets from a Personal Org (Beta)
Foster a collaborative environment for data exploration and analysis by sharing assets organized in
workspaces. This enables colleagues to view, interact with, and contribute to the content you create.
Personal Org Considerations (Beta)
With your Personal Org you have complete control over all assets you create. However, access to
reused assets will continue to be governed by existing access policies, and settings.

Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
You can access data for creating visualizations and dashboards by connecting to data directly (Data 360
DMOs and CIOs), by connecting to a semantic model that already has a data connection, or by
uploading your own data from CSV files. Use Tableau Semantics within Tableau Next to create a semantic
model and semantic calculated dimensions and measures.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Adding Data to a Tableau Next Workspace
You can analyze any data that is available in your workspace. You can access data from an existing data
model object (DMO) in Data 360, connect to a semantic model that has a data connection, or upload
your own data from files or cloud-based resources. Changes you make in Tableau Next don't affect the
source data in Data 360 or in your original files.
Create Semantic Models and Define Metrics
Tableau Semantics within Tableau Next gives you the power to create semantic models and then build
metrics upon them. You can also use semantic models to create calculated fields in your visualizations.
Adding Data to a Tableau Next Workspace
You can analyze any data that is available in your workspace. You can access data from an existing data
model object (DMO) in Data 360, connect to a semantic model that has a data connection, or upload
your own data from files or cloud-based resources. Changes you make in Tableau Next don't affect the
source data in Data 360 or in your original files.

Comma-separated value (CSV) A text file that stores data in a table-like format.
CSV files are commonly used to move data
between applications and programs. The file can
be up to 10 MB. For more information on what’s
supported for CSV files, see Supported File
Formats in Data 360.
Excel (.xlxs) A file that stores data in spreadsheet format. .xlsx
files are used for data organization, analysis, and
visualization. The file can be up to 10 MB. For
more information on what’s supported for .xlxs
files, see Supported File Formats in Data 360.
Data model object (DMO) A Data 360 metadata object that describes the
structure and schema of data stored in one or
more data lake objects.
Data lake object (DLO) Contains the raw data ingested into Data 360 or
referenced from an external system, such as
Snowflake. A DLO can contain both raw data and
data that is calculated during ingestion.
Calculated insight object (CIO) A data model object created after a calculated
insight is processed. You can connect to an
existing CIO in Data 360 and add it as a data asset
in your workspace.
Semantic model A collection of metadata that describes a set of
data stored in Data 360. A semantic model can
have one or more data objects that describe the
grouping of semantic definitions. It can also
include metrics and calculated insights.
Add an Existing Data Asset to a Tableau Next Workspace
You can add existing assets such as data connections, data model objects, dashboards, and semantic
models, to new or existing Tableau Next workspace.
Upload Data to a Tableau Next Workspace Using a CSV File
Add your own data to a new or existing workspace using a CSV file. The data is parsed, verified, and
added to a new data object. You can then use it in a semantic model, visualization, or dashboard.
115

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
Upload Data to a Tableau Next Workspace That Use an Excel File
Convert local Excel data into native Data Cloud assets for semantic models and visualizations. Tableau
Next supports Excel files up to 100 MB and 500 columns.
File Upload Processing Modes and Limits
Upload processing modes in Tableau Next are based on file size and column count thresholds. Files
exceeding standard limits are automatically queued for asynchronous processing, optimizing resource
allocation without impacting platform stability.
Connect to Data Sources with the Connector Catalog
Centralize your data management by building a curated library of data connectors within the new
Connector Catalog. This unified interface shows all supported platforms alongside their specific
connection features and requirements.
Review Data Uploaded to Tableau Next
After you upload an existing data asset or a CSV file to a Tableau workspace, you can review the fields
and data types, along with up to 100 rows of data in each field. After reviewing, you can use newly
uploaded data immediately for models or visualizations.
Adding Assets to a Data Space
A data space acts as a logical partition for organizing your data. By default, the system places your
assets in the default data space. However, if other data spaces are available in Data 360, you can add
assets to one of them.
Rename Data Source Columns in Your Workspace
Optimize data clarity by renaming data source columns to align with your organization's business
terminology. Modifying these field names in your workspace ensures that you interact with familiar
labels during analysis.
Search for Data Tables and Schemas
Efficiently navigate data structures by searching for schemas and tables across all active connections in
Tableau Next from the search bar. Scan your data sources and see matches that preserve their original
folder hierarchy and context.
Turn on Acceleration for Data Connections
Data acceleration improves query performance by temporarily caching data in Data 360. Acceleration
is off by default when you add an external database connection. You can turn it on by selecting Full
Refresh or Incremental Refresh.
Update Data Lake Objects with Excel and CSV File Refreshes
Keep your analytics current by replacing existing data sources with updated Excel or CSV files directly
within the Data Lake Object. This streamlined upload process maintains your established data
structures while ensuring users always work with the latest records.
Date and Datetime Formats in Tableau Next
Tableau Next recognizes and supports multiple date and datetime formats.
Add an Existing Data Asset to a Tableau Next Workspace
You can add existing assets such as data connections, data model objects, dashboards, and semantic
models, to new or existing Tableau Next workspace.

Review Data Uploaded to Tableau Next
After you upload an existing data asset or a CSV file to a Tableau workspace, you can review the fields
and data types, along with up to 100 rows of data in each field. After reviewing, you can use newly
uploaded data immediately for models or visualizations.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To upload data and create visualizations in Tableau Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next: Next Platform Analyst permission set
131

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
11.. Review the column headers to make sure that the metadata is clear and accurate.
22.. Check that the sampled data matches what’s expected in each column, including the data type.
33.. Save the uploaded data and decide next steps.
••
To finalize the uploaded data and return to the workspace, click Save.
••
To model the metadata associated with the data, click Create a Semantic Model.
••
To visualize the data, click Create a Visualization. Because a visualization is based on a semantic
model, Tableau generates a basic semantic model to create the visualization.
Adding Assets to a Data Space
A data space acts as a logical partition for organizing your data. By default, the system places your assets
in the default data space. However, if other data spaces are available in Data 360, you can add assets to
one of them.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Data spaces enable you to categorize your data, metadata, and processes, such as by brand, region, or
department. This categorization can control user access, ensuring users only see and work with data
relevant to their specific category. Also, you can merge and analyze data across different data spaces.
To reassign a Data Lake Object to a different data space, from your workspace open an external data
source. Select Customize Data Lake Object. Type in a new name in the top field. Click Create.
Note While changing the data space, use this opportunity to rename any columns in the data
source before saving.

Search for Data Tables and Schemas
Efficiently navigate data structures by searching for schemas and tables across all active connections in

Turn on Acceleration for Data Connections
Data acceleration improves query performance by temporarily caching data in Data 360. Acceleration is
off by default when you add an external database connection. You can turn it on by selecting Full Refresh
133

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
or Incremental Refresh.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
When you turn on acceleration while creating an external database connection, you turn on caching.
Caching prevents the risk of potential timeouts or at run time latencies while accessing data from an
external source.
You can incrementally refresh your data as frequently as every 15 minutes. An incremental refresh
updates the cached data with data that's been added or changed since the last refresh, but it doesn’t
update deleted data. If the refresh frequency is 24 hours or more, you can then select the time and day.
You can also retrieve data in full refresh mode. If you opt for a full refresh, during each refresh cycle, all
the existing cached data is deleted and replaced with the new dataset. To keep your data current and in
sync with your data source, perform a full refresh at regular intervals.
Note Enabling acceleration might impact the consumption of credits used for billing.
Turn on Data Acceleration
11.. Open an existing Data Connection in your workspace and select the source data you want to update.
22.. Click Create Data Lake Object.
33.. Click Edit under the Accelerate Data section to turn on acceleration.
44.. Configure your cache refresh method:
aa.. For a Full Refresh: Select this option and use the Frequency menu to select how often the data
updates.
bb.. For an Incremental Refresh: Select this option, then select a date field in the Record Modified Field
and set your preferred frequency.
55.. Click Refresh data now to start the acceleration process.
66.. Click Update Acceleration to save your changes.
Update Data Lake Objects with Excel and CSV File Refreshes
Keep your analytics current by replacing existing data sources with updated Excel or CSV files directly
within the Data Lake Object. This streamlined upload process maintains your established data structures
while ensuring users always work with the latest records.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
134

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To upload data into Tableau Next: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set
Tableau Next automatically optimizes performance by selecting the most efficient processing method
based on file size and columns. For more information, File Upload Processing Modes and Limits.
11.. Open to the Data Lake Object for the table you want to update.
22.. Click Replace Data…
33.. Click Upload File and select your updated Excel or CSV file.
Important Files are limited to 10 MB for Excel files and 100 MB for CSV files for refreshes.
44.. Review the data for formatting, columns, and that sample records appear correctly.
55.. Click Save to update your existing data table.
Date and Datetime Formats in Tableau Next
Tableau Next recognizes and supports multiple date and datetime formats.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To change the data format in Tableau Next: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set
A field with date or datetime data must be assigned a date and time format. This format is used when
parsing the data and writing to the data lake object (DLO).
Tableau Next determines the applicable data type and format by reviewing a sample of the incoming raw
data. You can override the data type for file-based connections (.csv and .xlsx files), but it is your
responsibility to review your data and assign the appropriate data type and format. In the case of date
and datetime data, if you choose to override the data type, make sure the format stays the same so that
it still reflects the format in the raw data.
Overrides are only available for file-based connectors (.csv and .xlsx files).
Modify Date or Datetime Format
If you need to modify the format for a date or datetime field, you must do this before you save your data
in a data lake object (DLO).
135

11.. After uploading your data, use the action menu for the table you want to modify and select Customize
Data Lake Object.
22.. In the preview of the data, click the down arrow next to the column whose format you want to change.
33.. From the Data Types list, select Date or Date & Time.
The current format is displayed.
44.. Click the down arrow to see other possible formats or to manually enter the format.
55.. Click Save to update the format.
66.. Click Save to open the Create Data Lake Object dialog.
77.. To save the DLO to a data space other than the default data space, click Edit and select the data space
or data spaces in which you want to save the DLO, then click Assign Data Spaces.
If you don't see any data spaces other than the default data space, none have been set up in Data
Cloud or you do not have access to them.
88.. Click Create to create a DLO with your data and the updated date or datetime format.
Supported Date and Datetime Formats
Tableau Next supports multiple date and datetime data types.
A field with date or datetime data must be assigned a date and time format. This format is used when
parsing the data and writing to the data lake object (DLO).
Tableau Next suggests the applicable data type and format by reviewing a sample of the incoming raw
data. You can override the data type by customizing the object prior to its creation, but it is your
responsibility to review your data and assign the appropriate data type and format. If you choose to
override a date type to a datetime or a datetime to a date type, make sure the format stays the same so
that it still reflects the format in the raw data.
Overrides are only available for file-based connectors (.csv and .xlsx files).
The table shows the date and time pattern strings and their definitions. Use this table when validating
the date and time format suggested for each field. The patterns are case-sensitive and failure to
differentiate between cases can produce vastly different results. For example, “MM” is used to represent
the month while “mm” represents the minute.

Create Semantic Models and Define Metrics
Tableau Semantics within Tableau Next gives you the power to create semantic models and then build
143

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
metrics upon them. You can also use semantic models to create calculated fields in your visualizations.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Semantic Models in Tableau Next
With semantic models, your data becomes unified into a single source of truth, accessible throughout
Salesforce. Semantic models provide a way for your data to be interpreted consistently across
Salesforce by mapping it to standardized business terms and logic. Semantic models are first-class
Salesforce metadata, so they’re available throughout Data 360 to power analytics and data-driven
experiences.
About Metrics
A metric is a business KPI that you can track over time and use to assess performance or business
processes. A metric is derived from one or more measures. You can apply a sentiment to a metric (for
example, up is good or bad), aggregation (for example, sum, non-cumulative, average, minimum,
maximum), and other business contexts.
Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta)
Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta) helps data teams prepare their Semantic Models for use with
AI agents such as Concierge: Analytics Q&A. It evaluates a semantic model's quality, identifies
inefficiencies, and offers guided resolutions to improve model reliability for AI-powered analytics.
AI-Generated Descriptions in Semantic Models (Beta)
Quickly draft comprehensive descriptions for semantic data objects by using Einstein. This input is
crucial for conversational analytics, giving AI agents the context they need for accurate discovery and
insights.
Use Q&A Calibration to Improve Accuracy in Concierge (Beta)
When Q&A Calibration is enabled in Tableau Next, analysts and data experts can test and verify
questions and answers for Concierge based on the selected analytics agent and its associated
semantic model. Through evaluating questions to determine if questions are accurate (verified) or
inaccurate, analysts can provide valuable input that your analytics agents can use to respond
consistently and accurately to semantically similar questions.
Semantic Models in Tableau Next
With semantic models, your data becomes unified into a single source of truth, accessible throughout
Salesforce. Semantic models provide a way for your data to be interpreted consistently across Salesforce
by mapping it to standardized business terms and logic. Semantic models are first-class Salesforce
metadata, so they’re available throughout Data 360 to power analytics and data-driven experiences.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
144

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
You can create semantic models from scratch, or you can build them off of an existing semantic model.
Either way, once you have a semantic model, you can customize it to suit your specific needs. For
example, you can rename fields, override relationship definitions, or even create your own metrics.
Semantic models also include a query generator, which allows you to leverage your data to answer key
business questions. The query generator sends customizable semantic queries based on your semantic
models, so you can get the insights you need quickly and easily.
When you create a semantic model, relationships that were mapped between data model objects
(DMOs) in Data 360 are included. This includes relationships that were previously created between
objects outside the Semantic Model Builder. Relationships that create circular relationships (relationships
that reference the same object multiple times) are removed.
There are two ways to add semantic models in Tableau Next. You can either create a new semantic
model, or select a semantic model based on an existing asset.
You can also create semantic models directly in Data 360. For more information, see Create a Semantic
Model in Tableau Semantics.
You can also use the Tableau Semantics connector for Tableau Desktop and Cloud to connect and use
semantic models in Data 360 just like you can in Tableau Next. For more information, see Salesforce
Help: Tableau Semantics Connector.
Add Semantic Models
Within Tableau Next, create a new semantic model from an existing asset.
Generate a Semantic Model with Data Pro (Beta)
Use Data Pro to build ready-to-use semantic models faster. Describe your business goals in natural
language, and Data Pro identifies relevant data objects from your workspace, generates necessary
relationships and business logic, and creates your model's structure and calculated fields. You can
then skip manual mapping to review and refine a completed model in minutes.
Accelerate Semantic Model Creation with Marketplace Templates
When developing semantic models to unify and make sense of your enterprise data, you can build a
model entirely from scratch or use a pre-configured template. Tableau Next Marketplace semantic
model templates are designed to accelerate your development process.
Create a Relationship with Suggest Relationships
You can use Artificial Intelligence (AI) to get relationship suggestions and create relationships in
Tableau Semantics. Suggestions are based on semantic objects and sample data added to your model.
Create a Calculated Field with Einstein
You can use Einstein to easily and effectively create calculated fields within Tableau Next.
Create Calculated Fields with Data Pro
Data Pro helps you create and refine calculated fields by chatting in natural language. It’s available
through the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce. This template uses the prebuilt Data
Pro: Semantic Model Curation topic.
145

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
Add Semantic Models
Within Tableau Next, create a new semantic model from an existing asset.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To create semantic models in Tableau Next: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set
11.. From your workspace, click Add and select New Semantic Model.
22.. Select the data model object that you want to build relationships on, and click Select.
Note You can only select one data object at a time.
33.. To add another data object, under Data Objects, click the plus icon.
Note For performance reasons, avoid mixing data objects with a Live Query Federation conection
and data objects from other sources.
44.. If you want to connect the data objects based on a common field, you can create a relationship. You
can either do this manually, or you can let Einstein suggest relationships for you.
55.. Click Show Suggestions and then review the suggestions and apply if needed. You can always turn
suggestions on or off using the Suggested Relationships toggle.
146

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
66.. Continue building your model by adding more data objects, calculated fields, parameters, and logical
views. For more information, see Semantic Model Builder in Tableau Semantics.
77.. To add a name and description to your semantic model, click the Settings icon.
88.. To test your model and make sure the semantic model works as expected, click Test Model. Tableau
Semantics runs your model through the Tableau Semantics Query Generator and gives you test results.
For more information, see Test a Semantic Model.
99.. After you’re finished creating a semantic model, you can now create a metric or a visualization. For
more information, see Add a Metric to a Semantic Model and Create Visualizations.
Generate a Semantic Model with Data Pro (Beta)
Use Data Pro to build ready-to-use semantic models faster. Describe your business goals in natural
language, and Data Pro identifies relevant data objects from your workspace, generates necessary
relationships and business logic, and creates your model's structure and calculated fields. You can then
skip manual mapping to review and refine a completed model in minutes.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Note Generate a Semantic Model is a pilot or beta service that is subject to the Beta Services Terms
at Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer, and
applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of this pilot or beta service is at the customer's
sole discretion.
View supported editions.
After you turn on Data Pro, follow these steps to generate a semantic model:
11.. Select a workspace that includes data.
22.. From the Assets list, select your semantic model.
147

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
33.. From the Add menu, select Semantic Model.
44.. Under Create New, select From Data Object.
55.. Click Build with Data Pro.
66.. Describe your analytics goal in detail using natural language. You can add the metrics, data, and key
performance indicators (KPIs) your business needs. Then, select Suggest Objects.
77.. Review the data objects identified by Data Pro and refine the list by selecting or deselecting items to
align with your business goal.
88.. After you review the data objects, click Create.
Data Pro builds the model in the background, including the generation of relationships and business
logic. You can perform other tasks while the build is in progress and return to the model later.
Limitations
These limitations apply to semantic model generation:
•
The suggested data objects are from your current workspace. To get the best results, verify that
your workspace includes all the objects you need for your analysis.
•
This feature is available only for workspaces with up to 100 data objects.
•
The feature currently supports only your default data space. Before you begin, verify that your
data objects are in this space and available for your model.
Accelerate Semantic Model Creation with Marketplace Templates
When developing semantic models to unify and make sense of your enterprise data, you can build a
model entirely from scratch or use a pre-configured template. Tableau Next Marketplace semantic model
templates are designed to accelerate your development process.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
These out-of-the-box templates are designed to decrease the time you spend on foundational data
architecture and complex data mapping. They provide a comprehensive, ready-to-use structure that
includes:
•
Pre-Mapped Data— mapping of essential data fields to common business concepts, eliminating the
tedious and error-prone process of manual mapping.
•
Defined Data Model Objects (DMOs)— establishing key objects, like Customer, Account, and Order,
with best-practice attributes.
•
Established Relationships— pre-configuring the logical connections and dependencies between these
data model objects, ensuring data integrity and correct aggregation paths.
•
Pre-Built Metrics— defining and calculating commonly required business metrics, like Total Sales,
Service Case Count, and Customer Lifetime Value.
•
Calculated Fields— essential computed fields necessary for deeper analysis, saving you the effort of
148

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
scripting formulas.
With Tableau Next Marketplace templates, you can bypass the heavy lifting of initial setup and data
structuring. Analysts, data architects, and business users can focus immediately on higher-value tasks,
like analysis, customization, and deriving insights. These templates focus on high-priority areas such as
Customer 360 data for a complete view of your customer, or specialized data for sales and service
performance monitoring.
Access your org’s Tableau Next Marketplace from any workspace when you add a new semantic model.
The Marketplace contains prebuilt semantic model templates that install semantic models, metrics,
calculated fields, and other data assets.
Configure the C360 Semantic Model
The Tableau Next C360 semantic model template offers a unified perspective of your data by
consolidating metrics from various Customer 360 features, such as Sales, Service, Marketing, and
Commerce. The template installs the semantic model and 36 pre-configured metrics, including open
pipeline, open and closed cases, email delivery and open rates. These detailed insights are designed to
support program managers and stakeholders. By providing a clear breakdown of performance across
multiple dimensions, the template allows effective progress monitoring, the identification of areas
needing improvement, and the execution of data-driven decision-making.
Configure the Sales Insights Semantic Model
The Tableau Next Sales Insights semantic model template offers comprehensive visibility into your
sales data. It includes key metrics for analyzing sales, team performance, leaderboards, stage
progression, conversion rates, and sales lead analysis. This template installs the semantic model along
with 15 pre-configured metrics and over 70 calculated fields. Designed for sales managers and key
stakeholders, these insights provide a detailed breakdown of performance and forecasting across
multiple dimensions, empowering them to monitor sales progress, pinpoint areas for improvement,
and make data-driven decisions.
Configure the Service Insights Semantic Model
The Tableau Next Service Insights semantic model template offers a comprehensive look at your
service data with metrics for cases, customer satisfaction, Omni-Channel, service agents, and
knowledge. Designed for service managers and stakeholders, this template delivers detailed
breakdowns of CSAT and case escalation across various dimensions. By installing the semantic model,
you immediately gain access to 26 pre-configured metrics and 50 calculated fields. These insights are
essential for continuous service progress monitoring, identifying areas that need improvement, and
driving data-informed decisions.
Configure the Service Agent Analytics Semantic Model
The Tableau Next Service Agent Analytics semantic model template is designed to enhance service
agent performance and conversation metric tracking. It helps you optimize agents by providing a
tailored set of data. The template installs the semantic model, 14 pre-configured metrics, and over 30
calculated fields. These insights offer a detailed analysis of agent sessions across various dimensions,
enabling data-driven decisions, improving agent quality, and identifying specific areas for agent
improvement.

Create a Relationship with Suggest Relationships
You can use Artificial Intelligence (AI) to get relationship suggestions and create relationships in Tableau
Semantics. Suggestions are based on semantic objects and sample data added to your model.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To use Suggest Relationships: Data Cloud Admin or Data Cloud for Marketing
Admin or Data Cloud Marketing Data Aware
Specialist
Before you begin, turn on Data Pro - Semantic Model Curation in Tableau Next Setup. Also, turn on Turn
on Einstein under Einstein Setup. Turning these features on also turns on Draft with Einstein.
11.. In Tableau Semantics, select a data model.
22.. To see relationship suggestions, click Show Suggestions.
After suggestion mode is turned on, the Suggested Relationships toggle is enabled automatically. You
can turn it on or off as needed.
Relationship recommendations appear with a sparkle icon between objects within the Semantic
Model Builder.
33.. To create a relationship, click the sparkle icon between two objects in the canvas builder.
The Recommended Relationship window appears and shows the objects and object fields between
which the relationship is formed.
44.. To edit the relationship before applying it, click Review.
The bottom panel opens. Here you can review the recommendations and edit any section you wish
within the relationship.
55.. To create the relationship based on the recommendation, click Apply.
The relationship is created and a straight line appears between the objects signifying a relationship.
66.. To reject the relationship recommendation, click Reject. You won't see this specific suggestion again.
Functional Limitations
155

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
For best results with relationship suggestions, keep these limitations in mind.
•
Suggestions are provided only for fields defined as primary and foreign keys. Composite (compound)
keys aren’t supported.
•
Supported fields include numbers and text fields up to 50 characters long.
•
The feature doesn't currently support Shared Dimension or Logical View data objects.
•
Performance and accuracy have been tested on models with up to 30 tables and 50 columns.
•
The feature is currently available only in English.
Create a Calculated Field with Einstein
You can use Einstein to easily and effectively create calculated fields within Tableau Next.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To use Suggest Relationships: Data Cloud Architect or Data Cloud for Marketing
Admin or Data Cloud Marketing Data Aware
Specialist
Before you begin, turn on Data Pro - Semantic Authoring AI in Data 360 Feature Manager. Also, turn on
Turn on Einstein under Einstein Setup. Turning these features on also turns on Relationship
Suggestions.
11.. In Tableau Next, select a data model.
22.. From the New dropdown, select Calculated Field.
33.. Under Draft this Calculated Field with Einstein, enter a description of what you want the field to
calculate and how. The more descriptive you are, the better!
Example: Create a calculated field that shows return on advertising spend for all campaigns, using
orders data.
44.. Click Draft with Einstein.
55.. Einstein automatically fills out all of the fields within the calculated field. An icon appears above each
field indicating it was generated with Einstein.
66.. Review the fields and formula and edit any section according to your needs and then click Save. The
new calculated field is added to the Semantic Model and you can now use it to create relationships.
See Also
Create Calculated Fields With Data Pro
156

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
Create Calculated Fields with Data Pro
Data Pro helps you create and refine calculated fields by chatting in natural language. It’s available
through the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce. This template uses the prebuilt Data Pro:
Semantic Model Curation topic.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Describe the calculated field you need to Data Pro. It asks questions to resolve ambiguities. For example,
if two fields have similar names, the agent asks which one to use. Once you save the suggestion, Data Pro
creates the calculated field to fit your analytical needs.
Differences Between Draft with Einstein and Analytics Visualization Agent
With both Data Pro and the built-in AI experience, you describe the field you need by using natural
language. But the draft with Einstein is a one-turn experience. If you want to make changes, you must
start over. With Data Pro, you can have a conversation with the agent. You can answer questions and
refine your answers. Data Pro then creates the most suitable calculated field. Also, keep in mind that
Draft with Einstein and Data Pro need different licenses.
Technical Requirements and Limitations
Review these requirements and limitations to understand Data Pro's current capabilities and to verify it
functions correctly.
•
Data Model Relationships: To create a calculated field, a predefined relationship must exist between
data model objects. The agent can't create calculations for data objects without an existing
relationship.
•
Semantic Model Dependency: Data Pro's suggestions use the structure, metadata, and relationships
defined in your semantic model. The suggestions are based on the information available when you
make a request.
•
Supported Formulas: Data Pro formulas uses only the syntax and functions that Tableau Next
supports.
•
System Dependencies: Data Pro depends on the broader Analytics and Visualization template being
active in your organization.
•
Supported Languages: Data Pro supports only English at this time.
Tips for Getting the Best Results
For the best results, provide clear requests and verify your semantic model is well defined. The quality of
the agent's suggestions depends on the clarity of your request and the state of your data model.
157

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
•
A well-defined data model leads to better results. Data Pro bases all its suggestions on the existing
structure, metadata, and relationships in your semantic model.
•
Answer the agent's clarifying questions. If a request is ambiguous, Data Pro asks for more detail to
provide the correct result. For example, it asks you to choose between two fields with similar names.
•
Rephrase your request if it fails. If Data Pro can't create a field from your description, it presents a
message asking you to change your description and try again.
Data Pro and Trust
Data Pro operates with data security as a core principle. When you request a calculated field, the agent
sends contextual information from your semantic model to the Large Language Model (LLM). This
information includes the model's schema and metadata. To protect sensitive data, Data Pro uses the PII
masking feature of the Einstein Trust Layer, which masks personally identifiable information before
sending it to the LLM. Data Pro operates only within the context of your current semantic model.
Set up Data Pro
Data Pro helps you create and refine calculated fields in Tableau Next by chatting in natural language.
You engage in a conversation with the agent, answer its clarifying questions, and refine your answers
to create the most suitable calculated field. Data Pro is available through Tableau Next by using the
Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce.
Create a Calculated Field with Data Pro
Data Pro streamlines the creation of calculated fields through a conversational process. You describe
your requirements using natural language, and the agent interacts with you to generate the ideal field
for your needs.
Set up Data Pro
Data Pro helps you create and refine calculated fields in Tableau Next by chatting in natural language.
You engage in a conversation with the agent, answer its clarifying questions, and refine your answers to

To set up Data Pro: Marketing Intelligence Admin permission set
To set up Data Pro for your users, follow these steps.
158

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
11.. Turn on Einstein.
aa.. From Setup, find and select Einstein Setup.
bb.. Turn on the Turn on Einstein toggle. See Set Up Einstein Generative AI.
22.. Turn on Data Pro.
aa.. From Setup, find and select Tableau Next Features.
bb.. Turn on the Agentforce for Analytics toggle.
cc.. Turn on the Data Pro .
33.. Create a new agent in Agentforce. If this was already done, you don’t need to redo it.
aa.. From Setup, in the Quick Find box, enter and select Agentforce Agents.
bb.. Ensure that Agentforce is on, and then select New Agent.
cc.. Select Analytics and Visualization and then click Next.
44.. Add the Data Pro topic.
aa.. Select Data Pro - Preparation and Modeling and then click Add.
bb.. Click Next.
cc.. Customize your agent by adding a role and company information. Make sure you have a useful
name and a unique API name. Click Next.
dd.. Select the Language and Tone. Click Next.
55.. Activate the agent.
aa.. After adding the topic, activate the agent.
bb.. If you get an error on the page warning you that your changes aren't being saved, disregard it.
66.. Grant user access
aa.. Set up Agent Access Control to grant your users access to use the agent. If this was already done for
Concierge, it doesn't need to be redone for Data Pro.
bb.. From Setup, in the Quick Find box, enter and select Permission Sets.
cc.. Click New to create a permission set. Name it Agent Access and click Save.
dd.. Still in the Agent Access permission set, click Agent Access.
ee.. Click Edit, select your desired agent (you named it in step 3c), use the arrow to add it to Enabled
Agents and click Save.
ff.. Still in the Agent Access permission set, click Manage Assignments.
gg.. Click Add Assignments and assign the agent to the users who should be able to access it.

Take Action on a Record
About Metrics
A metric is a business KPI that you can track over time and use to assess performance or business
processes. A metric is derived from one or more measures. You can apply a sentiment to a metric (for
example, up is good or bad), aggregation (for example, sum, non-cumulative, average, minimum,
maximum), and other business contexts.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
In some cases, a metric has a behavior that is applied across all queries. For example, GDP is a metric
that can only be calculated on a country level, and it should be calculated this way in different views of
data.
Sub-metrics are detailed performance indicators that provide additional granularity to a main metric.
160

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
They allow you to break down primary metrics into more specific, actionable data points, offering deeper
insights and more precise analysis.
You can store metrics and reuse them across different experiences, while using semantic queries to
visualize metrics over time.
Metrics can also be created directly in the Semantic Layer tab in Data 360. For more information, see
Create a Metric for a Semantic Model.
Add a Metric to a Semantic Model
Within Tableau Next, create a metric based on a semantic model.
Use Metric Templates from Marketplace (Beta)
Creating effective metric insights is essential for monitoring performance and driving business
decisions. You have two primary options for building these insights, developing custom key
performance indicators (KPIs) from scratch or by using a pre-configured metrics template. Tableau
Next metric templates offer a significant advantage by decreasing the time required to construct your
KPIs.
Add a Metric to a Semantic Model
Within Tableau Next, create a metric based on a semantic model.

33.. To create a metric, from the New dropdown, select Metrics.

The metric is now available to use in dashboards, to follow, and to embed in web pages.

Creating effective metric insights is essential for monitoring performance and driving business decisions.
You have two primary options for building these insights, developing custom key performance indicators

These templates are designed to provide you with prebuilt, high-value KPIs. They’re tailored to integrate
with your business data, focusing either on the comprehensive Customer 360 data view or on more
specialized data related to sales and service performance. This approach helps you to deploy industry-
standard and best-practice metrics without the extensive development and configuration overhead
associated with custom creation.
Access your org’s Tableau Next Marketplace from any workspace when you add a new metric. The
Marketplace contains prebuilt metrics templates that install the supporting semantic models and the
metrics.

The template checks your org to make sure that all prerequisites and permissions are set up correctly to
install the metrics. If the template validation is successful, the installation process begins. If it fails, you’re
provided with a list of errors to resolve.

Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta)
Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta) helps data teams prepare their Semantic Models for use with AI
agents such as Concierge: Analytics Q&A. It evaluates a semantic model's quality, identifies inefficiencies,
and offers guided resolutions to improve model reliability for AI-powered analytics.
This feature helps data analysts achieve clarity on what makes a semantic model "AI-ready" and provides
targeted guidance on where to start and what to prioritize. In addition to diagnosing problems, the tool
offers suggested improvements.
Note Semantic Model AI Optimization is a pilot or beta service that is subject to the Beta Services
Terms at Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer,
and applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of this pilot or beta service is at the
Customer's sole discretion.
Use of this service might consume Customer Data Cloud Credits or Flex Credits.
How It Works
Semantic Model AI Optimization runs a series of automated checks against your semantic model and
calculates an overall model health, which is categorized as Low, Moderate, or High. The assessment
168

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
covers several key areas:
•
Missing Description: Checks whether descriptions are needed in custom objects and semantic assets.
•
Missing Relationships: Flags objects with disconnected relationships, as this can impair an AI agent's
ability to reason across the model.
•
Similar Meanings (Beta): AI helps identify semtnaitc definitions that are so similar, it's hard for both
the system and dashboard designers to tell them apart. This can lead to inconsistent or random results
in your analytics.
The assessment presents results in a panel that visualizes the Model Health strength for you to explore
the rules, issues, and suggested resolutions. The feature also provides a contextual call-to-action button
for each rule, tailored to the resolution path. Acting on these suggestions improves your model’s
readiness for AI-powered agents and analytics.
What’s an AI-Ready Semantic Model?
An AI-ready semantic model is a data model structured specifically for AI comprehension. While
traditional semantic models were designed primarily for human understanding and dashboard creation,
AI agents require models with a high degree of precision, clarity, and consistency.
AI readiness in a semantic model means that AI agents can reliably interpret business context from it. An
AI-ready model has clear labels, complete descriptions, low ambiguity, and consistent metadata.
Without these elements, AI agents struggle to provide accurate output, which can slow AI adoption and
erode user trust. Data teams use the Semantic Model Optimization feature to prepare their models by
guiding them to focus on the highest-impact improvements needed for AI-driven use cases.
To learn more about semantic models and best practices for creating them, we recommend completing
the Design Your Semantic Model for Analysis Trailhead.
Enable Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta)
To begin using Semantic Model Optimization, an administrator must first enable it.
Using Semantic Model AI Optimization (Beta)
After a system administrator enables the feature, you can begin using it to evaluate and improve your
semantic models. You interact with the feature primarily through the Optimize Model Panel, which
visualizes the Model Health and AI-readiness strength of your models.
Resolve Overlapping Data Definitions with Similar Meanings (Beta)
Improve model clarity by proactively resolving similar definitions that confuse AI agents. The Similar
Meanings (Beta) feature uses AI to detect synonyms, acronyms, and semantic overlaps to prevent
ambiguity. By analyzing field labels, descriptions, and parent object semantics, the tool identifies when
a model contains "two names for one thing" or "the same name for different things"—concepts that
often lead to inaccurate agent reasoning. This deep scan allows you to distinguish between closely
related fields, helping agents correctly interpret your data and provide more reliable answers.

Run an Initial Scan

Resolve Similar Meanings
11.. On the Similar Meanings card, click Review to see the list of identified similar meanings.
22.. Review each identified similar pair.
••
Examine the flagged fields to determine if they represent "two names for one thing" (such as ARR
vs. Annual Recurring Revenue) or "the same name for different things".
••
Review the Classification Labels and Reason on each list item for further details on the identified
similarity and why the system identified the pair as similar.
Tip Use the provided reason as an indicator for potential issues and their resolutions.
33.. Distinguish the fields by taking one of these actions.
Clarify Field Label Provide a unique and clear display name.
Clarify Field Description Provide a distinguishing description, or use the
Description Generation feature.
Hide Field Set Field Visibility to False to hide the field from
analytics dashboards and AI agents.
Remove Field Navigate to the full builder properties view to
remove the field or update the table definition.
44.. Manage your progress by updating the Status of each item.
Unresolved Pairings you haven’t resolved.
Tip Filter for unresolved items to focus on

pending similar meanings instead of the full
list.
