Note We recommend completing the review of all identified similar meanings before initiating
another scan.
Limitations
Keep these considerations in mind when using the Similar Meanings feature.
Supported Metadata
•
Field and Object Metadata: Scans are limited to field and object Display Names and
Descriptions. While other properties provide context, the identification process focuses on these
two properties due to their significant weight in search algorithms.
•
Logical View Definitions: System logic does not evaluate implementation details within logical
views. The system treats a logical view as a flat object, analyzing only its display name,
description, and field definitions.
•
Metrics and Parameters: These semantic definitions are not currently scanned for similar
meanings.
Model Compatibility
•
PDS-Based Models: The tool scans PDS-based models for similar meanings, but users can only
edit the Description and Field Visibility to distinguish between fields.
•
Extended Models: Similar meanings are not supported for extended models. Neither base nor
extended objects are included in the scan.
Results and Reasoning
•
Clusters: If more than two fields are similar, the system may surface multiple pairs of similar
meanings within the same cluster.
•
Hierarchies: Fields representing a hierarchy (such as Category Level 1 and Category Level 2) can
sometimes be flagged as similar meanings. To help the agent distinguish between these levels,
provide clear descriptions, including examples of the values each level represents.

Use Q&A Calibration to Improve Accuracy in Concierge (Beta)
When Q&A Calibration is enabled in Tableau Next, analysts and data experts can test and verify
questions and answers for Concierge based on the selected analytics agent and its associated semantic
model. Through evaluating questions to determine if questions are accurate (verified) or inaccurate,
analysts can provide valuable input that your analytics agents can use to respond consistently and
accurately to semantically similar questions.

Terms at Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed by Customer,
and applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of this pilot or beta service is at the
Customer's sole discretion.
Semantic definitions are integral to the accuracy of the analytics agent. Every organization knows its data,
its semantic model, and its business preferences. The input your experts can provide through Q&A
Calibration helps to calibrate and tune your semantic definitions to fit the context of your organization.
176

Tableau Next Connect and Prepare Data in Tableau Next
Verified questions are saved in a repository with the related semantic model. When a user asks a
question in Concierge, the agent considers questions from the agent's semantic model question bank
and grounds the answer with relevant context. When your users ask similar questions, the agent can
respond consistently with verified answers.
Note The number of verified questions is limited to 100 questions per semantic model.
Because Q&A Calibration uses Concierge, you will need to confirm that Agentforce for Analytics and
Concierge: Analytics Q&A are enabled in Tableau Next settings. If you don't see these settings, to get
started with using the Analytics and Visualization Agentforce template, contact your Salesforce
representative.
11.. In Tableau Next, click Q&A Calibration.
22.. In the Agent search field, click and select an agent or enter the name of an agent and select the agent
from the list.
33.. In the question bank page, click Add.
44.. Select a semantic model, and then enter a question in the Concierge pane. The question is added to
the question bank list as New. You can also view the semantic query that is generated from the
question.
55.. Click a question in the list to open a preview in Concierge and prompt the analytics agent to analyze
the question.
66.. For Classification, select Verified or Inaccurate.
••
For Verified, click Mark as Verified to confirm. By selecting Verified, you are coaching the analytics
agent and Concierge with positive examples to train the agent for consistency and accuracy. This
saves the classification and opens the Calibration Suggestion pane with For suggestions on how you
can calibrate the answer. If a user asks the same or a similar question, that answer will be suggested
as part of the utterance to the agent.
••
For Innacurate, select a classification reason. Enter the answer you expected, and then click Save &

Suggest Calibration.
This saves the classification and opens the Calibration Suggestion pane with For suggestions on how
you can calibrate the answer.
For each suggestion you want to explore, click Calibrate. Tableau Next will provide options for
making adjustments in the semantic model.
If you select Calibrate, you'll need editing permission to access and edit the relevant semantic model.
77.. For Inaccurate responses, it's helpful to provide context about why the answer was inaccurate. The
following options are possible:
••
Unrecognized term in question
••
Additional fields need to be included in the answer
••
Wrong fields were used
••
Calculation not supported
••
Wrong calculation used
The Calibration Suggestion panel provides information on how to address the issue. For example, it
might suggest you create a new business preference, select a new field, or update a calculation. When
you click Calibrate in this panel, the recommended page opens.
After you save your changes, click the Home button to return to the Q&A Calibration page.
Each time you make adjustments for a calibration, retest the question to assess the resulting response.
You can view the semantic query that is generated from the question. You may want to calibrate a
question multiple times to get the response you expect.
Click Retest to test the question again after calibration and evaluate if the change affected the answer.
A yellow warning icon next to a question name indicates that you verified the question previously, but
the semantic model has changed since that time. You can retest the question to validate it again.
As you save and calibrate questions, ask the questions again using Concierge in Q&A Calibration.
You can manage the list of questions at any time by sorting, filtering, deleting, or adding questions.

Visualization Builder. Create visualizations or tables in your workspace that connect related data and
explore trends, and analyze performance. Use the results to draw conclusions about your business and
make informed decisions.
Work with Data Tables in Tableau Next
Use Data Table view in Tableau Next to review and compare exact values across records in a
structured, row-and-column layout. This view makes it easy to scan, sort, and validate data across
multiple fields without opening individual records. Data Table view also helps you identify duplicates
and anomalies at a glance.
Create a Visualization with Semantic Model Data
Build a Tableau Next visualization on your semantic model data to discover valuable insights about
your business. For example, create a visualization that shows sales patterns by product category and
sub-category.
Customizing Tableau Next Data Views with Marks Properties
Customize how your data appears and control the level of detail shown in your data views using Marks
properties. Apply colors that align with your data and preferences. Add labels to show exact values and
provide context. Or encode data by size for faster comparison of values. The marks available to you
depend on the data view you select.
Add Filters to a Tableau Next Visualization
Use filters to focus on a subsection of your data. For example, zero in on sales orders greater than
$10,000 in the Central region. You can apply filters to both measures and dimensions.
Evaluating Performance with the Analytics Pane
Interpret your data with advanced analytics tools in Tableau Next to support informed business
decisions. Apply forecasting models to historical data to project future outcomes and identify trends.
Use reference lines to compare metrics against benchmarks and evaluate performance in your

Visualize Data Intensity and Patterns in Heat Maps
In Tableau Next, use heat maps to compare measure values across related categories and quickly
locate where your data shows high or low concentrations. With color coding applied to a heat map,
identify key data correlations, such as the impact of web traffic on sales. Or highlight outliers, like a
low-engagement period on a top-selling product, so that you can address anomalies quickly. For
example, compare product subcategories across each region in a heat map to see which subcategories
drive the most profit in each region.
181

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
Create a Funnel Chart to Show Value Drop-Offs Across Stages
In Tableau Next, you can quickly surface patterns as your data moves through a process or workflow
with funnel charts. Compare conversion from one stage to the next and spot bottlenecks in business
operations to prioritize areas for improvement and efficiency gains. For example, analyze how
opportunities move through your sales stages in a funnel chart to pinpoint the stages where
opportunities need attention first.
Analyze Comparable Measures with Dual and Synchronized Axes
Use dual axes in Tableau Next to compare two measures on separate axes in the same chart across a
shared dimension. Because each measure retains its own scale, you can highlight patterns and trends
side by side while keeping the chart easy to interpret. To surface differences between measure values
more clearly, synchronize the axes to align them to a common scale. For example, in a chart, compare
sales and profit by product sub-category using dual axes. Then synchronize the axes to evaluate how
closely profitability aligns with sales.

Analyze Comparable Measures with Dual and Synchronized Axes
Use dual axes in Tableau Next to compare two measures on separate axes in the same chart across a
shared dimension. Because each measure retains its own scale, you can highlight patterns and trends
side by side while keeping the chart easy to interpret. To surface differences between measure values
more clearly, synchronize the axes to align them to a common scale. For example, in a chart, compare
sales and profit by product sub-category using dual axes. Then synchronize the axes to evaluate how
closely profitability aligns with sales.

Customizing Tableau Next Data Views with Marks Properties
Customize how your data appears and control the level of detail shown in your data views using Marks
properties. Apply colors that align with your data and preferences. Add labels to show exact values and
provide context. Or encode data by size for faster comparison of values. The marks available to you
depend on the data view you select.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Size Encoding in Tableau Next Visualizations
Use size encoding to highlight differences in values in bar, square, or circle mark types. You can base
mark size on the values of the continuous measure field represented by the marks. Or, base the mark
size on values of a different continuous measure field of your choice. Choose relative sizing to highlight
contrast between values or absolute sizing to to apply a uniform scale based on a fixed pixel range.
Combine size with color to interpret data easily
Personalize Charts with Custom Colors
Apply custom colors to a Tableau Next visualization to make them more engaging and easier to
interpret. Choose colors that align with your branding, provide visual cues about data patterns, or
highlight business priorities. You can set up to 10 custom colors in a chart. If a chart has more than 10
values, it reuses the colors in sequential order.
Personalize Charts with Custom Colors
Apply custom colors to a Tableau Next visualization to make them more engaging and easier to interpret.
Choose colors that align with your branding, provide visual cues about data patterns, or highlight
business priorities. You can set up to 10 custom colors in a chart. If a chart has more than 10 values, it
reuses the colors in sequential order.

Evaluating Performance with the Analytics Pane
Interpret your data with advanced analytics tools in Tableau Next to support informed business
decisions. Apply forecasting models to historical data to project future outcomes and identify trends. Use
reference lines to compare metrics against benchmarks and evaluate performance in your visualizations.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Forecasting with Tableau Next
Apply forecasting models on historical data to predict future outcomes that guide business decisions.
You can choose the forecasting model to use, set how far ahead to forecast, and define the time range
to base forecasts on. You can add forecasting to up to three measures in a single visualization. All
selected measures use the same forecast settings, such as model type, duration, and prediction
interval.
Applying Reference Lines to Tableau Next Visualizations
Use reference lines in Tableau Next to evaluate performance against benchmarks, such as customer
satisfaction (CSAT) scores or monthly sales targets. You can base the reference line on the same
measure, calculated field, or date field as the axis it’s on. You can also choose a different source field
to compare against another business metric. Define the reference line as a constant for fixed targets or
a computed measure value, such as average monthly revenue, to compare performance within
context. Personalize it with labels and colors to interpret data easily.
Forecasting with Tableau Next
Apply forecasting models on historical data to predict future outcomes that guide business decisions.
You can choose the forecasting model to use, set how far ahead to forecast, and define the time range to
base forecasts on. You can add forecasting to up to three measures in a single visualization. All selected
measures use the same forecast settings, such as model type, duration, and prediction interval.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
This table includes a description of the settings available with forecasting in Tableau Next.
Setting Description
Model
Select the forecasting model to apply.
187

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
Setting Description
•
The Holt-Winters model forecasts data with
changing trends over time and applies
smoothing to generate stable future estimates.
•
The Ridge Regression model forecasts data with
a mostly linear trend and applies regularization
to keep variable weights balanced and reduce
overfitting.
Forecast Periods Set the forecast duration based on the chart’s time
interval, such as days, weeks, or months.
Replace missing values (nulls) with zeroes Choose whether to fill missing data points with
zeroes in the forecast.
Ignore Last Specify the number of most recent periods to
exclude from the data used in forecasting, based
on the chart’s time interval.
Show prediction intervals Set the percentage for the shaded area where the
forecasted values are expected to fall. A higher
percentage produces a wider shaded range that
represents a higher likelihood that the actual
values fall within that range. For example, if you
select 95%, the model expects the actual values
to fall within the shaded area with 95%
likelihood.
Visualize Future Trends with Forecasting
In Tableau Next, you can add forecasts to charts that contain a date value and one or more measures.
Project sales for multiple segments of the same metric in a single chart, or related metrics across distinct
charts within one visualization. For example, create a visualization with projected total sales orders in one
line chart, and include another chart that breaks down the sales forecast by segment for a more targeted
comparison.

Tableau Next Forecasting: Limitations
Review these limitations before applying forecasting to Tableau Next visualizations.
•
Forecasting supports Date Values only.
•
Forecasting isn’t supported on charts that include reference lines.
•
Forecasting requires at least three data points at the aggregation and group levels you select. For
example, you need at least three months of data for monthly forecasting or three years of data for
yearly forecasting.
Applying Reference Lines to Tableau Next Visualizations
Use reference lines in Tableau Next to evaluate performance against benchmarks, such as customer
satisfaction (CSAT) scores or monthly sales targets. You can base the reference line on the same
measure, calculated field, or date field as the axis it’s on. You can also choose a different source field to
compare against another business metric. Define the reference line as a constant for fixed targets or a
computed measure value, such as average monthly revenue, to compare performance within context.
Personalize it with labels and colors to interpret data easily.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
This table includes a description of the settings available with reference lines in Tableau Next.

Setting Description
Add Line To Choose the field to draw the reference line on.
The line appears horizontally or vertically based on
the axis for that field.
Value Set the position for the reference line. Enter a
constant, or select a source field and the
calculation to compute the line’s position.
Label Choose the annotation for the reference line.
Show its value, add custom text, or leave it blank.
Line Color
Apply a color to the reference line. Choose a color
from the Default, Custom, or Data palette. Data
palettes provide context-aware options to match
the type of data you’re working with, such as:
•
Categorical to distinguish between distinct
groups
•
Sequential to highlight increasing or decreasing
values across a low-to-high range
•
Diverging to contrast values around a
meaningful midpoint, such as zero or an
average
•
Semantic to help users interpret data more
quickly using colors with standard, widely
understood meanings, such as red for negative
values and green for positive.
Add Reference Lines to Compare Data Points
In Tableau Next, you can add reference lines to highlight key thresholds in your visualizations. Add lines
at constant values, computed values, or values from other fields to compare performance against
business goals. For example, evaluate monthly sales against a median sales reference line and a
reference line based on another field, such as profit ratio. Also, highlight an important date with a
constant reference line, such as the start of a quarter.

Enhancing Your Analysis with Advanced Features
Analyze your data for different scenarios and compare outcomes using parameters. Use calculated fields
to define new values and answer targeted questions. Explore your data more efficiently across multiple
191

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
levels of detail with dimension hierarchies.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Add a Calculated Field to Your Data
Sometimes, analyzing your data source requires adding a calculated field based on your existing fields.
For example, you want to review the cost for each product that you sell. You can either create a
calculated field manually, or you can use Semantics AI and let Einstein draft one for you.
Use Parameters to Explore Data Dynamically in Tableau Next
Define parameters in Tableau Next to explore what-if scenarios in dashboards and see how different
inputs affect your business. Create a parameter on any field type and use it with a calculated field,
filter, or reference line to update the visualization interactively. Use a parameter list to switch between
fields or field values, such as countries, to compare each value’s impact separately. You can also create
parameters for values outside the dataset, such as discount percentages, to see how they affect
results.
Navigate Levels of Detail Efficiently with Dimension Hierarchies
Define dimension hierarchies in Tableau Next to move between high-level summaries and detailed
breakdowns in your analysis. Create hierarchies using related dimensions or date fields, and use them
in visualizations or tables to roll up or drill down data.

Add a Navigation Action to a Visualization
Embed navigation actions into your Tableau Next visualization. The navigate action allows users to
navigate to external sites with clickable links directly in a visualization data field.
Add a Salesforce Action to a Visualization
Add a Salesforce action to your Tableau Next visualization. Users can invoke these actions to create,
update, and interact with Salesforce records. For example, the actions you add let users create cases,
update accounts, or post to Chatter.

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
The Open Record action is designed for multi-org environments. When records with identical names
exist across different data sources, users are asked to choose the specific record they want to access,
which then opens in its corresponding source org.
Create Effective Dashboards with Tableau Next
The Tableau Next dashboard is designed to streamline your workflow by providing a clear, intuitive
overview of all the critical information you need. Whether you’re tracking performance metrics,
managing tasks, or monitoring key data, dashboards bring everything together in one place.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Analyze Critical Data with Dashboards
Tableau Next dashboards help you view and act on your data to make smart decisions. Whether you're
monitoring or analyzing business data, Tableau Next custom and templated dashboards help you
organize and understand the information that matters most to you.
Use Dashboard Templates from the Marketplace
When designing dashboards, you can start with a blank dashboard or you can use an out-of-the-box
dashboard template to decrease your design lift. Tableau Next dashboard templates provide layouts
tailored to your data and how you want to present it.
Add and Configure a Visualization Widget
Add visualizations to Tableau Next dashboards to enhance communication and collaboration by
presenting data in a clear and intuitive way. Use visualizations to help you effectively act on key issues,
such as drivers for sales growth.
Add and Configure a Metric Widget
Integrate real-time metrics into your Tableau Next dashboard for enhanced, data-backed decision
making. Displaying KPIs visually enables users to quickly assess trends and track progress.
Filter Data with the Toggle Widget
Toggle widgets enables you to quickly switch between different settings.
Filter Data with the List Widget
List widgets help users refine and control which data is presented, making it easier to analyze and
understand specific subsets of information.
Interact with Dashboard Filters
In Tableau Next, dimensions are categorical or qualitative fields, such as names, dates, or geographical
data. When you have a dashboard made of metrics and visualizations (vizzes), you can select one or
more marks to filter the dashboard to show only the selected data. And when you select marks to filter
data on the dasfhboard, the metrics and vizzes update to reflect the filtered data. For example, if your
dashboard has multiple vizzes that show sales by category and region, you can click region to filter the
dashboard to show data for only that region. Or you can select multiple categories and a region to
filter the dashboard by using different dimensions at the same time.
Personalize Dashboard Visuals with the Image Widget
199

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
Upgrade the visual impact of your Tableau Next dashboards by adding custom graphics, such as logos
and icons, to your workspace. You can upload image files directly from your local drive or select assets
from your existing library to foster brand recognition.
Set Initial Values for Your Dashboard
Select values to filter as your dashboard loads, ensuring that users see the most relevant information
right away. For example, you can set your dashboard to automatically display sales by quarter in EMEA.
Create and Share Custom Dashboard Views
Share precise dashboard information with users by creating custom views. These saved views allow
quick access to specific data configurations without needing to reset filters and settings repeatedly.
Define Filter Selections for Widgets
Dashboard widgets can be set to automatically receive or ignore specific filter selections.
Add Descriptive Text Using the Text Widget
Use text widgets to provide explanations and information to improve user understanding and
engagement.
Apply Conditional Formatting to Visualize Dashboard Data Performance
Improve your data visualization by adding conditional formatting to Tableau Next dashboards. Create
custom rules to automatically highlight important data, making it easier to analyze and interpret key
performance metrics. By applying custom text formatting, you can quickly identify areas of success or
those needing attention, which helps you make more informed decisions.
Add Single-Click Actions to Your Data
Create a data journey for your users by turning your static Tableau Next dashboard into an interactive
experience with single-click actions that lead to detailed pages or initiate Salesforce Flows. For
example, use actions to add dynamic navigation to guide sales managers from a summary dashboard
to pipeline details, progressively revealing more detailed information.
Add Efficiency to Your Dashboard with the Container Widget
Make widget tasks easier by adding them to a container widget. You can also configure containers with
single-click actions to add interactivity to your dashboard.
Customize Dashboard Widgets Using Parameters
Use parameters to repurpose dashboard widgets for various projects, saving development time and
effort.
Reuse Widgets Across Dashboards and Workspaces
Maintain design consistency and speed up your workflow by copying widgets across pages and
workspaces in Tableau Next. Use standard keyboard shortcuts to duplicate components while
preserving all custom formatting, colors, and styles.
Organize Content with Dashboard Pages
Make the information on a dashboard easier to digest by chunking the content into multiple pages.
And, with fewer queries per page, you can expect an increase in dashboard performance.
Adjust Dashboard and Widget Formatting
Format the dashboard and its widgets to create a consistent user experience.
Integrate Workspace Asset into Tableau Next Dashboards
Access metrics and visualizations directly through a convenient authoring panel to speed up your
dashboard creation. The integrated search bar helps you locate specific workspace assets, enabling
200

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
you to add high-value data into your dashboard layout.
Remove Dashboard Widgets
Remove widgets when you need to declutter your dashboard and remove outdated elements.
Preview and Save Dashboards
Previewing your dashboard is a critical step in the design and development process. It allows you to
thoroughly check the dashboard for data accuracy, visual clarity, and performance.
Simplify Dashboard Management
Duplicate a dashboard for testing purposes, remove an outdated version, or download a copy.
Analyze Critical Data with Dashboards
Tableau Next dashboards help you view and act on your data to make smart decisions. Whether you're
monitoring or analyzing business data, Tableau Next custom and templated dashboards help you
organize and understand the information that matters most to you.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To create and edit Tableau Next dashboards: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set
Dashboard templates come with prebuilt layouts that save time and follow best design practices. You can
add your own data and move assets around to make it your own. Custom dashboards give you even
more flexibility, enabling you to design your dashboard with advanced tools and interactive features.
Both types are easy to use, flexible, and work well on desktop and mobile devices.
Note To analyze data effectively on a dashboard, make sure that you have access to the necessary
data, visualizations, and metrics to perform comprehensive and accurate analyses.

tailored to your data and how you want to present it.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Available in:
Access your org’s Tableau Next Marketplace from any workspace when you add a new dashboard. The
Marketplace gives you the choice to create a blank dashboard or create dashboards from the prebuilt
layout templates. Any dashboard templates shared by other analysts are also available for selection.
These templates are designed to start working with semantic data models and their metrics. For more
information about creating models and metrics, see Create Semantic Models and Define Metrics. After
you create your dashboard, you can make any customizations you want, including adding visualizations,
actions, and other dashboard functionality.
Configure a C360 Metrics Overview Dashboard
The C360 Metrics Overview dashboard template provides a comprehensive overview of your key
performance indicators (KPIs) relevant to C360 features. The dashboard is designed for program
managers and stakeholders, offering a detailed breakdown of performance across various dimensions.
It enables progress monitoring, identifying areas for improvement, and making data-driven decisions.
203

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
Configure an Executive Overview Template
The Executive Overview dashboard template provides a high-level snapshot of critical business KPIs,
including revenue, profit margins, growth rates, risk management, and trend analysis. It is designed for
executives and senior leaders, highlighting monthly or quarterly trends, enabling data-driven strategic
planning, and performance reviews
Configure a F/Z Layout Dashboard
The F/Z Layout dashboard template is designed to show your data insights using proven eye-tracking
principles. The layout guides your users naturally through the dashboard content for maximized
comprehension. You can select the z-pattern layout for high-impact visuals or the f-pattern layout for
text-heavy content.
Configure a Performance Overview Dashboard
The Performance Overview dashboard template provides a comprehensive view of your key business
metrics, helping your teams track progress, identify opportunities, and mitigate risks. It emphasizes a
central hero metric, supplemented by supporting data and related metrics. The dashboard is designed
for sales, finance, service, and operations teams, delivering real-time insights.

Interact with Dashboard Filters
In Tableau Next, dimensions are categorical or qualitative fields, such as names, dates, or geographical
data. When you have a dashboard made of metrics and visualizations (vizzes), you can select one or
more marks to filter the dashboard to show only the selected data. And when you select marks to filter
data on the dasfhboard, the metrics and vizzes update to reflect the filtered data. For example, if your
dashboard has multiple vizzes that show sales by category and region, you can click region to filter the
dashboard to show data for only that region. Or you can select multiple categories and a region to filter
the dashboard by using different dimensions at the same time.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To share metrics, dashboards, and vizzes in Slack: Tableau Next Consumer (Unmetered) or Tableau
Next Consumer or Tableau Unmetered Self-Service
218

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
Consumer or Tableau Next Self-Service Consumer
permission set
11.. From the Tableau Next app, open a dashboard.
22.. Click to select a mark, such as text in a table or a bar on a chart. To select multiple marks, click and
drag your cursor across the marks. Or press and hold the Ctrl key (Windows) or Command key (Mac),
and then click multiple marks to select them.
33.. Select one of the highlighted marks again to clear the filters.
If a metric’s definition doesn’t contain the additional dimension that you’re using as a filter, then the
metric doesn’t update. In addition, date-part filters and expression-based filters that are created in
visualizations aren’t applied as filters to metrics in the dashboard. However, calculated fields that use
expressions that are part of the definition in the semantic model are applied to metrics in the dashboard.
Finally, when you open a metric that has dashboard filters applied, the dashboard filters are carried
through to the metric detail page.

Considerations for Conditional Format Rules:
•
When you set multiple conditions within a single rule, they’re all applied at the same time.
•
When conditions overlap, their formats will combine.
•
When rules conflict, the most recently created rule is applied.

use actions to add dynamic navigation to guide sales managers from a summary dashboard to pipeline
details, progressively revealing more detailed information.

Add a Navigation Action to a Dashboard

dashboards and dashboard pages with detailed information on the selected data.
Add a Salesforce Flow Action to a Dashboard
Add a Salesforce Flow action to a widget to execute flows using the Tableau Next dashboard context.
Flows empower your users to seamlessly create follow-up tasks, dispatch reminder emails, or refresh
records directly from their dashboard insights. For example, add a flow action that lets sales reps
create a follow-up task directly from a dashboard widget showing at-risk opportunities.

List Parameter Example
You’ve been asked to create sales visualizations with several groupings, such as Region and Segment.
Let’s reuse the existing visualization that you can update as grouping requirements change.
List Parameter Example
You’ve been asked to create sales visualizations with several groupings, such as Region and Segment.
Let’s reuse the existing visualization that you can update as grouping requirements change.

Tableau Next Create Visualizations and Dashboards in Tableau Next
11.. Create a list parameter and name it GroupUpdate.
22.. Type in the API names from the semantic model for Region and Segment in the Values column.
33.. Type in Region and Segment respectively in the Display Name column.
44.. Select Region as the Default Display Name.
Now you’re going to create a calculated field for updating the visualization’s grouping.
55.. Click New and select Calculated Field.
66.. Set Field Type to Dimension and Data Type to Text.
77.. Type Grouping in the Name field.
88.. Here’s a syntax example for the Formula field: IF [Parameters].[GroupUpdate] = "Region"
THEN [Superstore_Orders].[Region] ELSEIF [Parameters].[GroupUpdate] = "Segment"
THEN [Superstore_Orders].[Segment]END
99.. Click Validate to confirm the syntax, then click Save.
1100.. Open the dashboard with the sales visualization and add a list widget.
1111.. Click Add List then Parameters.
1122.. Select the GroupUpdate parameter then save the dashboard.
1133.. Preview the dashboard and try out the list widget which should now update the visualization’s
grouping.

Format a Dashboard
To format the dashboard, click Dashboard Properties to open the properties panel.
Grid Preset
Grid Preset makes dashboard design easier by providing a structured framework that assists with the
arrangement of dashboard widgets. Use the available layout patterns to help you achieve a professional
appearance while reducing manual adjustments. Layout patterns include:
•
Standard is a commonly used, well-structured layout that ensures uniformity across different
dashboards.
•
Fine organizes information in a smaller space, making it easier to find information.
•
Spacious helps make dashboards more visually appealing by providing generous spacing between
dashboard widgets.
•
Custom offers more flexibility, enabling you to choose how data is presented and arranged.
Additional Layout Properties
The table below provides details on the remaining dashboard properties for fine-tuning the dashboard,
ensuring it aligns with user preferences and corporate standards.
Property Description
Search Locates a dashboard asset.
Maximum Dashboard Width Sets the largest width that a dashboard can have.
Grid Background Color Sets the background color of the dashboard grid.
Gutter Color Sets the color between widgets in a dashboard.

dashboard assets.
Sets how much curve is applied to the corners of
Border Radius
dashboard assets.

Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Share your workspaces and assets with other users. Embed Tableau Next visualizations, metrics, followed
metrics, and dashboards into Lightning pages. Copy Tableau Next component code to embed Tableau
Next assets into third-party web apps. Transform your dashboards into standardized templates to list on
Marketplace, empowering collaboration, promoting standardization, and reducing duplicate efforts
across teams. Deploy your Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards from your sandbox
org into your production org using change sets and data kits.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Share and Embed
Share your workspaces and assets with other users. Use Lightning App Builder to embed Tableau Next
metrics, visualizations, and dashboards into Lightning pages. Copy Tableau Next component code to
embed Tableau Next assets into third-party web apps.
Build Templates for Your Tableau Next Marketplace
Transform your Tableau Next dashboards into standardized templates that can be listed on
Marketplace, empowering collaboration, promoting standardization, and reducing duplicate efforts
across teams.
Deploy Tableau Next Assets
Deploy Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards from your sandbox org to other
sandbox orgs and your production Data 360 home and companion orgs using change sets. You can
also deploy Tableau Next and Data 360 assets from sandbox to production home orgs using data kits.
240

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Using sandboxes allows you to develop, test, and train without compromising the data and apps in
your production org. Using change sets and data kits for deployments moves the customizations you
created and tested in your sandbox directly to your production org.
Share and Embed
Share your workspaces and assets with other users. Use Lightning App Builder to embed Tableau Next
metrics, visualizations, and dashboards into Lightning pages. Copy Tableau Next component code to
embed Tableau Next assets into third-party web apps.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Manage Access to Assets in Tableau Next
Manage access to workspaces, dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models in Tableau Next. Use
the share option to add or remove viewers or editors for an asset. Access to data model objects and
data lake objects is controlled in Data 360.
Bring Insights into the Flow of Work
Extend Tableau Next everywhere throughout your org by integrating Tableau Next metrics,
dashboards, visualizations, and followed metrics into Lightning pages and custom pages using
Lightning web components. Specifiy component properties and filter to customize how each insight
renders and interacts with the page.
Embed Tableau Next Assets in Your External Apps
Tableau Next embedded analytics brings Salesforce’s interactive and analytical capabilities to your
apps quickly. Tableau Next integrates with your existing technologies by using Salesforce
authentication and platform security, bringing insights into your flow of work and reducing context
switching for your users. For example, embed daily service metrics directly into your custom service
web app to give your technicians instant access to key information.
Manage Access to Assets in Tableau Next
Manage access to workspaces, dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models in Tableau Next. Use the
share option to add or remove viewers or editors for an asset. Access to data model objects and data
lake objects is controlled in Data 360.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Workspaces, dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models are, by default, private to the users who
create them. To let other users view or edit an asset, share it with them. You can also set the general
access for the asset so that all users in an org can view or edit the asset.
241

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
The access roles for an asset are viewer, editor, and owner. Viewers can interact with the asset, ask
questions about the asset and get answers, and see all the fields that the asset uses. Editors can modify
the asset. Owners can delete and share the asset.
Action Viewer Editor Owner
View and interact
See who has access
Modify and save
Share
Delete
The creator of an asset and users who reuse that asset have the owner role.
Access for semantic models
To access a semantic model, users must also have access to the data space that the model belongs to.
For information about adding users to a data space, see Assign a Data Space Permission Set to a Data
360 User.
Some semantic models extend existing semantic models. If the semantic model that you share extends a
base model, users can’t view that semantic model unless they have access to the base model.
By granting users access to a semantic model, you grant those users access to the metrics that are part of
that semantic model. You can't manage access for individual metrics.
Access for data model objects and data lake objects
Access to data model objects and data lake objects is controlled by tags and policies set in Data 360. For
more information about how data policies work with Tableau Next, see Data Governance in Tableau
Next. If users have access to a visualization but don’t have access to the data that the visualization is
based on, they can access the visualization URL, but they don’t see the visualization.
Share Assets in Tableau Next
Share and grant other users access to workspaces, dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models in
Tableau Next. You can grant individual users access to an asset, and you can set the general access for
an asset for all users in an org.
Remove Access for Assets in Tableau Next
Remove access to a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model so that users can no
longer view or edit that asset in Tableau Next. You can remove access for individual users, and you can
restrict the general access for the asset so that only users who are individually added can access the
asset.
Copy a Link to a Visualization or Dashboard in Tableau Next
242

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Get a link to a Tableau visualization or dashboard in its current or default view. The current view is the
visualization or dashboard as you see it, with any modifications you’ve made. The default view is the
visualization or dashboard without filters, selections, or other modifications applied.
Share Assets in Tableau Next
Share and grant other users access to workspaces, dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models in
Tableau Next. You can grant individual users access to an asset, and you can set the general access for an
asset for all users in an org.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To share and grant access: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set, Data Cloud
Data Aware Specialist permission set
Asset owners can share assets. If you don’t see the share option for an asset, you can't share it. Sharing
doesn’t override permissions set by your admin. If users don’t have access to Tableau Next, you can’t
share with them. If users don't have the permission set required to edit assets in Tableau Next, they can't
edit an asset, even if you grant them editor access.
For dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models, you can grant viewer access, and for workspaces,
you can grant viewer or editor access. Sharing a workspace automatically grants users access to the
assets in that workspace if you have permission to share those assets. The role that you grant for a
workspace is inherited by the dashboards, visualizations, and semantic models that are members of that
workspace.
For semantic models, object-level permissions provide an additional means of access. Users with Modify
All Records object permissions can share a semantic model, even if they aren’t the owner. Users with
View All Records object permissions can view a semantic model, even if it hasn’t been shared with them
in Tableau Next.
Share with individual users
11.. On a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model, click Share.
22.. On the Manage Access tab, enter the name of the user you want to share with, then select the user.
33.. For workspaces, select the Viewer or Editor role for the user. Only the Viewer role is available for other
asset types.
44.. Click Add.
55.. Click Done to close the share options.
Access to a semantic model requires access to the data space that the model belongs to and access to all
243

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
of the objects and fields used by the semantic model. For more information about adding users to a data
space, see Assign a Data Space Permission Set to a Data 360 User.
Sharing a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model doesn’t share the data model objects
and data lake objects that those assets depend on for their data. For information about access to data
model objects and data lake objects, see Data 360 Governance in Tableau Next.
Set general access for all users
General access is the default access to an asset for all users in an org. When you create an asset, general
access is restricted. Only users who you share with individually can access restricted assets. If you set the
general access for an asset to viewer, you can still grant individual users editor access. If you grant an
individual user viewer access and set the general access to editor, then that user has editor access
because the higher level of access takes precedence.
11.. On a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model click Share.
22.. On the Manage Access tab, for General Access, select the role for all users in the org. For workspaces,
select the Viewer or Editor role for the user. Only the Viewer role is available for other asset types.
33.. Click Done to close the share options.
Remove Access for Assets in Tableau Next
Remove access to a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model so that users can no longer
view or edit that asset in Tableau Next. You can remove access for individual users, and you can restrict
the general access for the asset so that only users who are individually added can access the asset.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To remove access: Tableau Unmetered Platform Analyst or Tableau
Next Platform Analyst permission set
Remove access for individual users
11.. On a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model, click Share.
22.. On the Manage Access tab, next to a user’s name, select the permission role.
33.. Select Remove Access, then click OK.
44.. Click Done to close the share options.
244

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Restrict general access for all users
11.. On a workspace, dashboard, visualization, or semantic model, click Share.
22.. On the Manage Access tab, for General Access, select Restricted.
33.. Click Done to close the share options.
On assets with restricted access, only the users listed under Users with Access can view the asset.

Get a link to a Tableau visualization or dashboard in its current or default view. The current view is the
visualization or dashboard as you see it, with any modifications you’ve made. The default view is the

RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS

USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED

Next Consumer or Tableau Unmetered Self-Service
Consumer or Tableau Next Self-Service Consumer
permission set
Before you share a link to a visualization or dashboard, make sure that the users you’re sending the link

under Users with Access on the Manage Access tab for the asset. For more information see, Share Assets
in Tableau Next.
11.. On a visualization or dashboard, click Share.
22.. On the Manage Access tab, click Copy Link.
33.. Select either Copy Link to Current View or Copy Link to Default View.

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Note Tableau Next Lightning web components are only supported for Lightning App Builder. They

Add a Tableau Next Visualization to a Lightning Page
Integrate Tableau Next visualizations into Lightning pages so users can explore visualizations and
identify data trends in their workflows. To embed a visualization component in the page, follow these
steps.
Add a Tableau Next Dashboard to a Lightning Page
Integrate your Tableau Next dashboards into Lightning pages to surface key insights into user
workflows. To embed a dashboard component in the page, follow these steps.
Add a Tableau Next Metric to a Lightning Page
Integrate your Tableau Next metrics into Lightning pages to bring specific insights directly to your
users. To embed a metric component in the page, follow these steps.
Filter Values for Embedded Visualizations, Dashboards, and Metrics
Create filters for embedded Tableau Next visualizations, dashboards, and metrics with the filter builder
to show relevant embedded content. You can use the builder to set dynamic or static values in the
filter when the visualization, dashboard, or metric renders. On a record page, you can also base filter
values on the page context, such as account ID, or specify static values so that the embedded content
shows only relevant data.
Add a Personalized View of Metrics That a User Follows with the Tableau Next Following
Component

Filter Values for Embedded Visualizations, Dashboards, and Metrics
Create filters for embedded Tableau Next visualizations, dashboards, and metrics with the filter builder
to show relevant embedded content. You can use the builder to set dynamic or static values in the filter
when the visualization, dashboard, or metric renders. On a record page, you can also base filter values on
the page context, such as account ID, or specify static values so that the embedded content shows only
relevant data.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Record Value
When you select Record Value as the filter value type on a record page, you are using a record value to
provide the filter value in context of the current record. This makes the filter dynamic to the record being
viewed by the user.
Example Filter the dashboard data where the City field in the data model equals the Billing City
value of the current record.
250

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Static Value
When you select Static Value as the filter value type on a record page, you are using one or more static
values for the filter value regardless of the user or the record context.
Example Filter the dashboard data where the City field in the data model equals Aberdeen or
Abilene.
251

Embed Tableau Next Assets in Your External Apps
Tableau Next embedded analytics brings Salesforce’s interactive and analytical capabilities to your apps
quickly. Tableau Next integrates with your existing technologies by using Salesforce authentication and
platform security, bringing insights into your flow of work and reducing context switching for your users.
For example, embed daily service metrics directly into your custom service web app to give your
technicians instant access to key information.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Use the Tableau Next Embedding SDK to integrate Tableau Next insights into your own web apps. The
SDK provides modern, secure methods of authentication so that users can interact with Tableau Next
features from your web app.
253

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
Tableau Next embedding provides access to the dashboard, visualization, and metrics Lightning web
components, bringing Salesforce platform functionality to your web app. Programmatically control these
components in your web app by using filters and event listeners, enabling interactive functionality.
For detailed information, see the Tableau Next Embedding SDK developer guide.

Tableau Next provides a quick start for embedding code directly from your dashboard, visualization, or
metric. The embed feature creates JavaScript component code for your selected asset, that you can
copy and use in your web app.

programmatically in your web app code.
Build Templates for Your Tableau Next Marketplace
Transform your Tableau Next dashboards into standardized templates that can be listed on Marketplace,
empowering collaboration, promoting standardization, and reducing duplicate efforts across teams.
Why Use Templates?
To Establish Best Practices and Governance: Analysts can create and share "gold standard" templates for
common analyses (e.g., sales performance, marketing campaign effectiveness, customer churn),
establishing consistency in reporting and adherence to organizational data governance standards.
Onboarding and Training: New data analysts can quickly get up to speed by using pre-built templates,
understanding how established data models are used and how insights are visualized within the
organization. Using pre-built templates reduces ramp-up time significantly.
Accelerate Project Delivery: Instead of rebuilding similar dashboards for different departments or clients,
255

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
analysts can leverage existing templates, customizing them as needed. Analysts can deliver analytical
solutions faster and take on more projects.
Showcase Expertise and Build a Reputation: Talented analysts can gain visibility and recognition by
creating and sharing highly effective and innovative templates that solve common business problems,
which fosters a sense of community and internal thought leadership.
Facilitate Collaboration: Analysts often work across different business units. Shared templates can bridge
knowledge gaps and enable more seamless data sharing and understanding between departments. For
example, a finance analyst can use a sales team's template to understand revenue drivers from their
perspective.
Streamline Ad-Hoc Requests: When business users need quick insights, analysts can often fulfill these
requests rapidly by adapting a relevant template rather than starting from scratch, increasing their
efficiency and responsiveness.
Encourage Innovation through Iteration: When analysts share templates, others can build upon them,
suggest improvements, or create variations, leading to a continuous cycle of innovation and refinement
of analytical assets.
Build Dashboard Templates for Your Marketplace (Beta)
The Tableau Next Marketplace allows analysts to share, customize, and deploy dashboard templates
across an organization, with the aim of improving efficiency and standardization. For example, after
you build a dashboard highlighting your sales pipeline data, templatize it to share with your team and
other sales teams.

Deploy Tableau Next Assets
Deploy Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards from your sandbox org to other
sandbox orgs and your production Data 360 home and companion orgs using change sets. You can also
deploy Tableau Next and Data 360 assets from sandbox to production home orgs using data kits. Using
sandboxes allows you to develop, test, and train without compromising the data and apps in your
production org. Using change sets and data kits for deployments moves the customizations you created
and tested in your sandbox directly to your production org.

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
You can also deploy Tableau Next assets using other standard Metadata API tools, like the Salesforce CLI.
For more information on developer tools, see the Tableau Next ISV/Developer Guide.
Tableau Next in a Sandbox
A sandbox is a copy of your Salesforce org that you can use for development, testing, and training
without compromising the data and apps in your production org. Tableau Next sandboxes are the same
as Data 360 sandboxes. Data 360 sandboxes contain only metadata. No data is replicated when you
provision Data 360 in a sandbox, even in partial copy and full copy sandboxes.
Use a sandbox to:
•
Test changes against copies of your production data and users.
•
Provide a training environment.
•
Test AppExchange packages or integrations.
•
Isolate customization and development work from your production environment until you’re ready to
deploy changes.
For more information, see Data 360 in a Sandbox Org.
To create a sandbox with Tableau Next, follow the steps on Create a Data 360 Sandbox. Then, enable
Tableau Next and create your analytics assets in the sandbox.
Deployment Considerations for Change Sets and Data Kits
Most Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards depend on semantic models for their
data. Semantic models and Tableau Next assets can’t be deployed in a single change list. You must
deploy semantic models using data kits.
Data 360 companion orgs don’t support data kits, meaning semantic models and other Data 360 assets
can only be deployed to home orgs. To deploy Tableau Next assets in a companion org, you must first
deploy your semantic models to your home org. Next, validate that the semantic models are synced to
the companion orgs. Then, you can deploy your Tableau Next assets to the companion orgs using a
change set.
Deploy Tableau Next Assets with Change Sets
Deploy your Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards using an outbound change set
to your target home and companion orgs.
Deploy Tableau Next Assets in a Data Kit
Deploy your Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards to your target home org with a
data kit. Data 360 semantic models, the primary data source for Tableau Next assets, must be
deployed to target home orgs using data kits.
See Also
Deploy Data 360 Changes Across Orgs

your target home and companion orgs.

View supported editions.

To create, edit, or upload outbound change sets: Create and Upload Change Sets

Through Metadata API Functions
Create Your Change Set
11.. From Setup, enter Outbound Change Sets in the Quick Find box, then select Outbound Change
Sets.
22.. To create a change set, click New.
33.. To add components, click Add.
44.. Select your Tableau Next assets, listed as AnalyticsWorkspace, AnalyticsVisualization, and
AnalyticsDashboard component types. Then, click Add to Change Set.
55.. Optionally, click View/Add Dependencies to add dependent components.
Dependent components rely on the existence of other components. Unless you’re certain that the
dependent components exist in every org this change set will be deployed to, add dependent
components to the change set.
Deploy Your Change Set to Your Target Org
11.. Upload your change set to the target org that you want to send the change set to.
22.. In the target org, from Setup, enter Inbound Change Sets in the Quick Find box, then select
Inbound Change Sets.
33.. Find your change set and click Deploy.

Outbound Change Sets
Inbound Change Sets

Deploy your Tableau Next workspaces, visualizations, and dashboards to your target home org with a
data kit. Data 360 semantic models, the primary data source for Tableau Next assets, must be deployed
to target home orgs using data kits.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS

Through Metadata API Functions

Create Your Data Kit
11.. In your sandbox org, go to Data Cloud Setup.
22.. Under Developer Tools, click Data Kits.
33.. Click New.
44.. For Data Kit Type, DevOps.
55.. Select your data space and name the data kit.
66.. Add Tableau Next assets using Visualizations, Dashboards, and Workspaces in the Data Kit Contents
list
When you select a visualization or dashboard, the corresponding workspace is automatically added to
the data kit.
77.. Add any other assets, like semantic models or other Data 360 objects.
88.. Review the Publishing Sequence.
You must ensure your assets reflect the following sequence, at a minimum:
266

Tableau Next Embed, Share, and Deploy Tableau Next
••
Semantic Models
••
Workspaces
••
Visualizations
••
Dashboards
This is due to the dependency sequencing for data kits. Visualizations have dependencies on semantic
models and workspaces, and dashboards have dependencies on visualizations. The default sequence
is based on each asset's creation date, which may not suitable in all cases. For more information, see
Create and Publish a Standard Data Kit.
Important If you don't review and update the publishing sequence, data kit deployment will fail.
99.. Save the data kit.
Create a Change Set for Your Data Kit
11.. From Setup, enter Outbound Change Sets in the Quick Find box, then select Outbound Change
Sets.
22.. To create a change set, click New.
33.. To add components, click Add.
44.. Select the Data Package Kit Definition component type.
55.. Select your data kit and click Add to Change Set.
66.. Optionally, click View/Add Dependencies to add dependent components.
Dependent components rely on the existence of other components. Unless you’re certain that the
dependent components exist in every org this change set will be deployed to, add dependent
components to the change set.
Deploy Your Change Set to Your Target Org
11.. Upload your change set to the target org that you want to send the change set to.
22.. In the target org, from Setup, enter Inbound Change Sets in the Quick Find box, then select
Inbound Change Sets.
33.. Find your change set and click Deploy.
Deploying installs the data kit in your target org.
Deploy Your Data Kit in Your Target Org
11.. In your sandbox org, go to Data Cloud Setup.
22.. Under Developer Tools, click Data Kits.
33.. Select your data kit and click Data Kit Deploy.
The deployment installs all assets included in the data kit.

Tableau Next Tableau Next Apps for Salesforce
Tableau Next Apps for Salesforce
Provide your teams with curated paths through your data, plus powerful tools for spontaneous, deep
explorations. With a Tableau Next App for Salesforce, you have a purpose-built set of analyses and
insights about a specific area of your business. Answer pressing questions, predict outcomes, and get
recommendations on the next best actions with insights infused directly within the flow of work in
Salesforce.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Note Depending on the app, specific licenses, permission sets, or permissions might be required.
Sales Insights
Sales Insights is a data-driven sales solution that unites Tableau Next dashboards and analytics with an
easy-to-visualize view of your sales pipeline. Close deals with confidence, set accurate forecasts, and
deliver predictable revenue growth. Sales Insights leverages Tableau Next and AI to deliver intelligent,
data-driven insights in the context where sales teams work.
•
Salesforce Help: Sales Insights
Agentforce Lead Nurturing
Formerly called SDR Agent Analytics, Agentforce Lead Nurturing lets you scale your sales team,
accelerate growth, and maximize your pipeline by nurturing prospects 24/7 with an AI-powered sales
agent. Agentforce Lead Nurturing works new or updated leads, contacts, and person accounts
automatically, sending customized intro emails to each prospect, responding to replies, and connecting
qualified leads with sales reps.
•
Salesforce Help: Agentforce Lead Nurturing
Service Insights
Service Insights is a data-driven solution that displays key contact center performance metrics.
Determine whether you're meeting your customer service goals with easy-to-understand data
visualizations. Service Insights includes Data 360 and Tableau Next. The combination of customer data,
key performance indicators (KPIs), and AI help your service team improve operations and reduce costs.
•
Salesforce Help: Measure Success with Service Insights
268

Tableau Next Tableau Next Apps for Salesforce
Field Service Intelligence
Field Service Intelligence is a data-driven analytics tool that displays field service performance metrics.
Determine whether you're meeting your field service operational performance goals with easy-to-
understand data visualizations. Field Service Intelligence includes Data Cloud and Tableau Next. The
combination of operational data, key performance indicators (KPIs), and AI help your field service team
improve operations and reduce costs.
•
Salesforce Help: Improve Customer Experience with Field Service Intelligence
•
Blog: Operate with Confidence: Transform Your Field Service with Agentic and Conversational Analytics
Agent Analytics
Analyze your AI agents’ performance within user and agent sessions. Agent Analytics is built on the
unified Session Tracing Data Model (STDM), which captures every event and interaction within a session
and logs it as individual events. You can query the logged events to gain insights and optimize the Agent.
Use the data from your Service Agent Analytics and Employee Agent Analytics dashboards to understand
agent performance, and determine whether you want to adjust topics and agent actions to improve user
satisfaction.
•
Salesforce Help: Agent Analytics
Marketing Intelligence
Marketing Intelligence in Marketing Cloud Next empowers marketers to improve customer experiences
across multiple channels and at scale. Marketing Intelligence simplifies data management by providing
automated connectors, a unified data model, and data enrichment. Maximize conversions and improve
campaign performance with AI-generated campaign summaries and cross-channel attribution.
•
Salesforce Help: Marketing Intelligence in Marketing Cloud Next
•
Trailhead: Explore Marketing Intelligence
C360 Semantic Model
The C360 Semantic Model serves as a central foundation to define and manage metrics derived from
standard Salesforce objects. Use the C360 Semantic Model to get unified and trusted insights when you
work across multiple Salesforce products to create cross-cloud KPIs. The C360 Semantic Model solves
the problem of fragmented data and metrics by offering a single model with a unified view across various
Salesforce clouds, such as Sales, Service, Marketing, and Commerce.
•
Salesforce Help: The C360 Semantic Model

Revenue Management Intelligence
Revenue Management Intelligence offers a comprehensive suite of Tableau Einstein dashboards to
assess revenue strategies and achieve cost-effective results. Integrated with Data Cloud, the dashboards
feature detailed data visualizations and analysis of key performance indicators, enhancing the efficiency
of revenue strategies. Revenue Cloud uses analytics across all functional areas of Revenue Cloud to
create a smarter, more efficient product-to-cash solution.
•
Salesforce Help: Revenue Management Intelligence for Revenue Cloud
Patient Support Program Analytics
Patient Support Programs (PSP) Analytics provides a complete solution, including Tableau Einstein
dashboards, to improve patient and program outcomes. The dashboards give program leads and patient
services reps the data-driven insights they need to optimize operational efficiency.
•
Salesforce Help: Patient Support Program Analytics
Disease Surveillance Analytics
Use Disease Surveillance to efficiently manage and track disease-related data. Create and manage
disease definitions for effective categorization. Monitor cases by recording patient details and tracking
progress from first contact to resolution. Capture detailed investigation information, including specimen
details, patient medication history, and exposure data. Schedule inspection visits for on-site evaluations.
Monitor critical patient information such as medication, risk level, transmission mode, and
demographics. Enhance public health responses by streamlining case management, improving response
times, and ensuring accurate data tracking.
•
Salesforce Help: Disease Surveillance Analytics
Retail Banking Insights
Gain comprehensive, role-specific data insights, across customers, deposits, leads, referrals, and branch
activity with the Retail Banking Insights dashboards. Monitor customer performance and engagement,
analyze banker activity, and track customer acquisition and conversion efforts. Empowers Retail Personal
Bankers and Branch Staff to make informed decisions and effectively prioritize their work.
•
Salesforce Help: Retail Banking Insights
Agentforce IT Service Intelligence
Monitor performance, identify trends, and make data-driven decisions with Agentforce IT Service
Analytics Dashboards. These Tableau-powered dashboards give your IT leaders and service teams the
data they need for continuous improvement.

CSI Score synthesizes complex customer signals into a unified, trusted score. Gain a comprehensive view

actionable insights. The app surfaces emerging risks, growth opportunities, and strategic priorities.
Leaders can align teams, act on predictive drivers, and make confident, outcome-driven decisions to
drive measurable growth.

Share Previews of Tableau Next Metrics, Dashboards, and Vizzes in Slack
There are a few ways to share previews of a Tableau Next objects in Slack:
•
Share directly from Tableau Next to Slack. From a Tableau Next metric, dashboard, or viz, click Share,
select the Share to Slack tab, and compose your message.
•
Paste a link to a metric, dashboard, or visualization (viz) into the Slack message composer for a
channel or a direct message (DM).
•
Paste a link to a metric, dashboard, or viz into a Slack canvas.
If your admin Connected Salesforce and Slack, then the preview appears automatically. If your Slack
account isn't connected to Salesforce or if your accounts use different email addresses, then you receive
a Slackbot message asking you to connect to Tableau Next. Select Connect to Tableau Next, and follow
the prompts. After you allow Tableau Next to connect to Slack, you see a preview of Tableau Next objects
shared in direct messages, channels, and canvases.
When you share a link to a Tableau Next object in Slack, a preview appears in the message composer
letting you know that you’re about to share data with your team. If you choose to include the preview
when sharing to a Slack channel that you’re a member of or a direct message, then the preview includes
a point-in-time snapshot of the data, which can be seen by anyone who can see the message. If you
don't want to share a preview, you can remove the preview from the message composer in Slack and
send only the Tableau Next link.
Previews of Tableau Next objects that you share in Slack respect your permissions as the message sender,
including any filters applied and any data governance policies applied to your user. For example, metrics
sent in Slack include the metric value, chart, insight summary, and a time stamp to make it clear when
272

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
the snapshot was captured.
Similar to sending a screenshot in Slack, anyone who can view the message can see the preview, even if
they’re not Tableau Next users. To ensure trust and to respect security preferences, users can’t query live
data within Slack unless they have the required permissions in Tableau Next and Data 360.
Note If you don't see a preview or receive the Slackbot message to connect to Tableau Next when
you share a Tableau Next link, then your admin needs to Enable Slack for Tableau Next. You can still
share Tableau Next links in Slack, but you won't see a preview.
Access Live Data in Slack
Metrics, dashboards, and vizzes shared into a Slack canvas can be embedded as a live preview or static
snapshot. From any preview shared in Slack, users who have access can open the side pane to see live
detailed data, loaded from your perspective.
273

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
For all live data previews, Slack and Tableau always load the data from the viewer’s perspective,
respecting each viewer’s own security and data permissions. Viewers can also open the metric,
dashboard, or viz in the Tableau Next app in Salesforce, where they can filter and explore more.
If a user views a preview of a live embedded preview in Slack but doesn’t have access to view the metric,
dashboard, viz, or its underlying data in Tableau Next, then they see a message stating that they don’t
have access to view the preview.
Explore Tableau Next in the Slack Side Pane
When a Tableau Next metric, dashboard, or viz is shared in Slack, users can click the preview to open a
side pane in Slack that shows details and live insights.
Find Related Slack Conversations for Tableau Next
When a Tableau Next metric, dashboard, or viz is shared in Slack, you’re able to browse other related
messages that you have access to in Slack. You can also view related conversations for metrics from
the Tableau Next app.
Share Previews of Tableau Next Metrics, Dashboards, and Visualizations in Slack
When you send previews of Tableau Next metrics, dashboards, and visualizations (vizzes) in Slack,
anyone who can see the message will see the preview, including any filters applied. Before sending,
make sure you’re not sharing sensitive data.
Add Tableau Next Metrics, Dashboards, and Visualizations to a Slack Canvas
When you paste a Tableau Next link into a Slack canvas, you can choose to display a preview of the
metric, dashboard, or visualiation (viz) as a card. Unlike sharing a Tableau Next link in a direct message
or channel, previews in Slack canvases regularly refresh data from each viewer’s perspective. And you
can see when the data was last refreshed at the bottom of the card in the canvas.
Ask Questions About Shared Metrics and Dashboards in Slack Using Agentforce for Analytics
When an agent based on the Analytics and Visualization template is installed and enabled in Slack,
you can use Agentforce for Analytics to ask questions and dive deeper into your shared Tableau Next
metric and dashboard data.

To use the agent in Slack: Agent user permission from Salesforce for the
relevant agent
To use Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A in Slack:
•
Salesforce and Slack must be connected.
•
Tableau Next and Slack integration must be enabled.
•
Agentforce for Analytics needs to be set up in Salesforce and Slack.
•
A Tableau Next metric or dashboard has been shared to Slack.
There are two ways to engage the agent:
•
Click Ask Agentforce for Analytics in a metrics card or dashboard that has been shared in Slack.
•
Mention the Analytics and Visualization agent name in a thread about a shared metric or dashboard in
Slack.
To ask questions in Slack:
11.. In Slack, open a metric or dashboard shared from Tableau Next.
22.. In the metrics card or dashboard menu, click Ask Agentforce for Analytics.
aa.. Enter a question in the Agentforce panel. For example, "Break down total sales this quarter by
278

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
product family."
33.. Or, in a Slack thread that includes a shared metric or dashboard, enter the @ symbol and the agent
name, for example @Analytics and Visualization. Enter a question after the mentioned agent,
for example: "@Analytics and Visualization Break down total sales by product family for this year." If
the named agent isn't in the channel, click Add Them.
aa.. The agent responds with a preview of the response to let you decide if you want to share it. Click
Share in Thread to reveal the response in the thread.
Note that the thread needs to include the metric or dashboard you are asking about to have context
for the data it is analyzing.
Your user permissions determine the data and agents that you can access. The analytics agent analyzes
your question based on the data for the metric and other data in the semantic model that the metric or
dashboard is built on.
For more examples of the types of questions you can ask the analytics agent, see About Concierge:
Analytics Q&A in Tableau Next.
