Explore Metrics and Insights
Every metric includes one or more insights that you can explore to learn more about your data. When
more insights have been detected for a metric, you can click suggested questions in the Discover
Insights panel to open those insights.
Filter a Metric in Tableau Next
By adding filters to a metric in Tableau Next, you’re able to focus on the data that is most important to
you.
Download a Metric Snapshot
Download a snapshot image of a metric to a .png file. The snapshot is taken while running the item
with your filters and the latest data.
Copy a Tableau Next Metric Image to Your Clipboard
Copy a snapshot image of a metric to your clipboard. From your clipboard, you can then paste the
image for quick sharing in presentations, documents, or wherever you need it. The snapshot is taken
while running the item with your filters and the latest data.
279

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
About Metric Insight Summaries
Tableau Next displays a summary of the insights related to those metrics. Tableau Next creates the
insight summary differently, depending on whether Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics
Q&A is enabled or not. When the analytics agent is off, insight summaries are created based on the
metrics you follow (two or more required). When the analytics agent is enabled, Tableau Next creates
insight summaries using Agentforce (no followed metrics needed).
About Following Metrics in Tableau Next
After you follow a metric, the metric appears on the Following page in Tableau Next. While analysts
can find Following in the left panel, users with consumer permissions see the Following tab as their
home page web experience in the Tableau Next app.
Create Goals and Set Thresholds with Tableau Next
With Tableau Next, you can track important metrics and receive updates right where you’re working in
Salesforce or in Slack. When you set a goal in Tableau Next, you can also track a metric’s progress
toward that goal. And you can set thresholds to add context to your metrics. For example, if you have a
metric that tracks sales over time, you can set a goal and turn on forecasting to monitor your progress
toward that goal. And you can add labels for ranges such as “at risk,” “healthy,” for more context. Or set
thresholds independently of goals, which is particularly valuable for noncumulative metrics. For
example, you could set a threshold that indicates that it’s time to investigate when a metric is less than
a specified value.
Change the Time Range for Tableau Next Metrics You Follow
You can set a default time range for individual Tableau Next metrics, and you can set a dynamic time
range for all metrics that you follow.
Receive Inspector Proactive Data Alerts in Tableau Next (Beta)
Your users can ask Agentforce to send them Inspector data alerts when a metric meets specific
conditions. Inspector checks the conditions up to once per day, and if the conditions are met, a
notification is sent to channels chosen by your admin. To enable data alerts, add the Data Analysis and
Data Alert Management topics to the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce Builder, and
then choose which channels (desktop and mobile) the alerts are sent to.
Explore Metrics and Insights
Every metric includes one or more insights that you can explore to learn more about your data. When
more insights have been detected for a metric, you can click suggested questions in the Discover Insights
panel to open those insights.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
Note When Agentforce for Analytics and Concierge: Analytics Q&A are turned on in Tableau Next,
you can explore metric details and dashboards through natural conversation in the Agentforce
panel. For more information, see About Concierge: Analytics Q&A and Ask Questions with
Concierge: Analytics Q&A.

About Metric Insight Summaries
Tableau Next displays a summary of the insights related to those metrics. Tableau Next creates the
282

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
insight summary differently, depending on whether Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics
Q&A is enabled or not. When the analytics agent is off, insight summaries are created based on the
metrics you follow (two or more required). When the analytics agent is enabled, Tableau Next creates
insight summaries using Agentforce (no followed metrics needed).
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To explore metrics and insights: Tableau Next Consumer (Unmetered) or Tableau
Next Consumer or Tableau Unmetered Self-Service
Consumer or Tableau Next Self-Service Consumer
permission set
When Agentforce for Analytics with Concierge: Analytics Q&A is enabled in Tableau Next, metric insight
summaries are automatically generated in your preferred language and don't require followed metrics.
These insight summaries are available in the metric details page and for the top ranked insight in a
metric card.
Note Insight summaries might consume flex credits when generated using Agentforce for Analytics
with Concierge: Analytics Q&A.
When the analytics agent is off, Tableau Next leverages a large language model (LLM) to summarize the
most significant changes, considering Period Over Period Change or Unusual Change insights detected
by the system. The system prioritizes summarizing metrics with higher-impact insights (for example,
metrics with unusual change are ranked higher).
The resulting insight summary provides a personalized overview in plain, natural language with rich text
reference on the metric names to be found at the top of the Following page. To maximize consistency
and performance, insight summaries are generated up to once every 24 hours upon your last homepage
visit and cached for subsequent visits within the day.
The insights used for summaries are generated using natural language templates with values calculated
using deterministic statistics. The prompt used to summarize these factual insights is thoroughly tested
and consistently iterated.
Metric insight summaries are built on the Einstein Trust Layer, enabling trusted, ethical, and open AI-
powered experiences without compromising data security and privacy. Like all generative AI features in
Salesforce, users can also provide optional feedback on output they find to be biased, inaccurate, or
inappropriate to help to improve the experience.
Note For metric insight summaries that don't use the analytics agent, administrators need to enable
the Metric Insight Summaries org preference and the TableauFollowMetrics user permission, which
are included in the Tableau Next platform license and available when enablement steps have been
283

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
followed for the metric consumption experience. For more information, see Enable Metric Insight
Summaries in Tableau Next.

find Following in the left panel, users with consumer permissions see the Following tab as their home

Create Goals and Set Thresholds with Tableau Next
With Tableau Next, you can track important metrics and receive updates right where you’re working in
Salesforce or in Slack. When you set a goal in Tableau Next, you can also track a metric’s progress toward
that goal. And you can set thresholds to add context to your metrics. For example, if you have a metric
that tracks sales over time, you can set a goal and turn on forecasting to monitor your progress toward
that goal. And you can add labels for ranges such as “at risk,” “healthy,” for more context. Or set
thresholds independently of goals, which is particularly valuable for noncumulative metrics. For example,
you could set a threshold that indicates that it’s time to investigate when a metric is less than a specified
value.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
USER PERMISSIONS NEEDED
To create goals: Tableau Next Consumer (Unmetered) or Tableau
Next Consumer or Tableau Unmetered Self-Service
Consumer or Tableau Next Self-Service Consumer
permission set
When you create a goal or threshold, the goal and thresholds are visible to anyone who views that
metric. And all users can create, edit, or delete a goal or thresholds for a metric.
When you create a goal or threshold, it’s based on the metric’s current filters. Goals and thresholds also
use the time range selected by the user. For example, if the metric’s time range is quarter to date, then
the goal and thresholds set are from the beginning of the quarter to the end of the quarter.
When the goal’s time period has passed, the goal and thresholds don’t recur. If you have recurring goals
and thresholds, then set them at the beginning of each time period.
286

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
Create Goals and Thresholds for Tableau Next Metrics
When you set a goal, you see your progress toward that goal on your Tableau Next Following page. And
thresholds give more context to your metric's value. For example, you can create thresholds for
performance ranges, such as "healthy," "at risk," and "critical." You can set thresholds with or without
goals. And you can also set goals without thresholds.
Edit or Delete Tableau Next Goals or Thresholds
Editing a goal or deleting a goal or threshold changes them for all users who follow that Tableau Next
metric.

Change the Time Range for Tableau Next Metrics You Follow
You can set a default time range for individual Tableau Next metrics, and you can set a dynamic time
range for all metrics that you follow.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Set a Default Time Range for Individual Tableau Next Metrics
You can change the time range analyzed for a Tableau Next metric that you follow by setting a default
time range. After setting a default time range, the metric uses that time range everywhere you see
metrics that you follow—on your Following page and on mobile.
Set a Dynamic Time Range for All Followed Tableau Next Metrics
You can change the time range for all Tableau Next metrics that you follow simultaneously. Going
forward, all metrics that you follow use this time range everywhere you see metrics that you follow—on
your Following page and on mobile. Future metrics that you follow won't reflect this update. You can
undo this action by updating metrics individually.

Receive Inspector Proactive Data Alerts in Tableau Next (Beta)
Your users can ask Agentforce to send them Inspector data alerts when a metric meets specific
conditions. Inspector checks the conditions up to once per day, and if the conditions are met, a
notification is sent to channels chosen by your admin. To enable data alerts, add the Data Analysis and
290

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
Data Alert Management topics to the Analytics and Visualization template in Agentforce Builder, and
then choose which channels (desktop and mobile) the alerts are sent to.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Note Inspector Proactive Data Alerts in Tableau Next is a pilot or beta service that is subject to the
Beta Services Terms at Agreements - Salesforce.com or a written Unified Pilot Agreement if executed
by Customer, and applicable terms in the Product Terms Directory. Use of this pilot or beta service is
at the Customer's sole discretion.
Enable the Inspector Data Analysis and Data Alert Management Topics for Tableau Next (Beta)
Add the Data Analysis and Data Alert Management topics, so Agentforce can analyze Tableau Next
metrics and send you alerts about them.
Choose Where to Receive Inspector Proactive Data Alerts for Tableau Next (Beta)
Admins can choose which channels data alerts are sent to. When a user asks Agentforce to send them
an alert when a metric meets a specified condition, Inspector sends a notification to the channels
selected.
Create an Inspector Proactive Data Alert for a Tableau Next Metric (Beta)
Get proactive alerts about important changes to your Tableau Next metrics. Ask Agentforce to send
alerts for specific events, such as when a metric exceeds a certain value or a metric is outside of the
expected range. After you ask Agentforce to alert you about a metric, Inspector data alerts are sent to
channels set by your admin, such as desktop and mobile.
Manage Inspector Proactive Data Alerts for Tableau Next Metrics (Beta)
When you receive a data alert, you can view the alert, manage the alert, or ask a follow-up question by
clicking the notification. Depending on the channels chosen by your admin, you can receive alerts to
desktop and mobile. After you create an Inspector data alert, you can delete the alert if you no longer
want to be notified about these types of events.
Get Inspector Proactive Data Alerts for Tableau Next (Beta) in Slack
When you create Inspector data alerts, your alerts appear in the notifications menu in Tableau Next.
And after you install the Agentforce app in Slack, you also receive Inspector alerts in Slack. For
example, you can ask Agentforce to notify you when a metric reaches a specified value or increases by
a specified percentage. Or you can ask Agentforce to alert you every day about a metric’s value. When
the condition is met, Inspector sends an alert to Tableau Next and Slack with insights about what
contributed to the change.

Work with Tableau Next Visualizations
Take action on records directly from visualizations. Download snapshot images of visualizations for your
projects and to collaborate with your team.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
Take Action from Your Tableau Next Visualization
When actions are enabled for a visualization, you can click on a record field to view the available
actions.
Download a Snapshot of a Visualization
Download a snapshot image of a visualization to a .png file. The snapshot is taken while running the
item with your filters and the latest data.
Take Action from Your Tableau Next Visualization
When actions are enabled for a visualization, you can click on a record field to view the available actions.
RREEQQUUIIRREEDD EEDDIITTIIOONNSS
View supported editions.
You can take actions on any record field with a blue label in the visualization. Click the label and then
select an action from the menu.
The action menu can include:
•
URL navigation to Salesforce pages and external websites.
297

Tableau Next Work with Tableau Next
•
Object-specific actions and global actions to create, update, and interact with Salesforce records. For
example, you can open records, create cases, update accounts, and post to Chatter.
The action list is specified by the visualization creator. Only actions that accessible for the current page
layout and user permissions are visible. In Summer '25, the list of available actions is limited to 5 actions.
The Open Record action is designed for multi-org environments. When records with identical names
exist across different data sources, select the record you want to access, opening it in the corresponding
source org.
